# Unfinished SMP Work — pre-SM10 completeness audit of `docs/planning/`

> **Status**: OPEN — the register of what is *not* finished in WS-SM.
> **Audited cut**: `v0.34.2` (HEAD `1d44001`).
> **Scope**: every plan in `docs/planning/` **except**
> [`HARDWARE_PARTITION_ISOLATION_PLAN.md`](HARDWARE_PARTITION_ISOLATION_PLAN.md),
> which was explicitly excluded from this audit.
> **Question asked**: is the project ready to begin SM10
> ([`SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md`](SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md)), the v1.0.0
> release-closure phase that makes the kernel bootable on Raspberry Pi 5?

## 1. Verdict

**Not yet.** Three findings block *starting* SM10, and SM10's own plan
materially understates its scope. This is a scoping-and-registration
problem rather than a "the proofs are missing" problem: the substance of
SM0–SM9 is real and the tree is healthy.

Tree health at the audited cut, measured rather than asserted:

| Check | Result |
|-------|--------|
| `lake build` | 436/436 jobs, exit 0 |
| `./scripts/test_smoke.sh` (Tier 0–2) | exit 0 |
| `./scripts/test_full.sh` (Tier 0–3) | exit 0 |
| `sorry` / `axiom` in `SeLe4n/` + `tests/` | zero |
| `./scripts/check_version_sync.sh` | 36/36 sites |
| `CLAUDE.md` vs `AGENTS.md` | byte-identical bodies |

Tier 4 is the exception, and it is the reason this audit exists — see §2.1.

### 1.1 Per-plan verdict

| Verified status | Plans |
|-----------------|-------|
| **complete** (1) | `WS_RC_R4_TYPE_LEVEL_PROMOTION_PLAN.md` |
| **complete with registered debt** (8) | `REPLY_OBJECTS_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`, `SMP_DECLASSIFICATION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`, `SMP_FOUNDATIONS_PLAN.md`, `SMP_INFORMATION_FLOW_PLAN.md`, `SMP_PER_CORE_SCHEDULER_PLAN.md`, `SMP_PER_OBJECT_LOCKS_PLAN.md`, `SMP_TLB_SHOOTDOWN_PLAN.md`, `SYSCALL_RETURN_ABI_PLAN.md` |
| **incomplete** (10) | `IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md`, `SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md`, `SMP_FINE_LOCK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`, `SMP_MULTICORE_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`, `SMP_PANIC_HANG_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md`, `SMP_PER_CORE_STATE_PLAN.md`, `SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md`, `SMP_RUST_HAL_PLAN.md`, `SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`, `SMP_VERIFIED_LOCK_PRIMITIVES_PLAN.md` |

Two of those ten warrant immediate qualification:

- **`SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md` has been re-scoped pre-v1.0.0.**
 The audit read its post-v1.0.0 framing as deliberate and therefore not a
 blocker. That framing has since been rejected as a maintainer decision:
 shipping a verified microkernel whose core concurrency primitive carries a
 known-deferred completeness story understates what "verified" means on the
 one component every other subsystem's serialisability argument rests on.
 Its residue is now owned by WS-RR phase RR6
 ([`SMP_RELEASE_READINESS_PLAN.md`](SMP_RELEASE_READINESS_PLAN.md)) — the lock
 phase, not the boot-path phase that precedes it — and the plan's own header
 records the re-scoping.
- **`SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` is "incomplete" by construction** — SM10
 has not started. It is marked incomplete here because its *own scope
 statement* is wrong (§2.2), not because its work is undone.

## 2. What actually stands between HEAD and a bootable kernel

### 2.1 The acceptance gates never ran — closed at v0.34.2

Every one of the fourteen QEMU sub-tests behind
`scripts/test_tier4_smp_bootcheck.sh` exited `0` when its prerequisites
were missing; `run_check` scored that as `PASS`; and the tier signed off
with `All checks passed`. `qemu-system-aarch64` was installed by neither
`scripts/setup_lean_env.sh` nor any CI workflow, so on the nightly runner
all fourteen skipped and the tier passed — meaning the SM1, SM3, SM5, SM6
and SM7 hardware acceptance criteria **had never executed anywhere**, while
`SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` §2 lists "Acceptance gates for SM0..SM9
green" as an SM10 dependency.

**Closed at v0.34.2.** QEMU is now a test dependency in
`setup_lean_env.sh` (with dnf/yum/pacman name mapping) and installed
synchronously in the `nightly-tier4` job; `test_lib.sh` gained
`SELE4N_SKIP_EXIT` (77) and `run_gate_check`, so a gate that cannot run is
recorded **NOT RUN** and named in the summary instead of scored PASS;
`SELE4N_REQUIRE_GATES=1` promotes a skip to a hard failure, which is the
mode SM10.1's release validation must use; and
`scripts/test_gate_skip_accounting.sh` pins the mechanism as a Tier 0
witness, verified to fail against the pre-fix tree.

**Still owed.** Installing the emulator removed one of the two skip causes.
All fourteen gates now skip on `SELE4N_KERNEL_IMAGE env var not set`, which
no amount of installing fixes — it needs the bootable image owed by
SM10.1.1. The tier now reports that fact rather than concealing it, but
**the SM0–SM9 acceptance criteria remain unexecuted**, and SM10 may not
treat that dependency as met until they run.

### 2.2 SM10.1 is a bare-metal Lean runtime port, not a version bump

`SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` §1 scopes the phase as "All substantive SMP
work is complete; SM10 synchronizes documentation, completes the test
suites, bumps the version", at a 4–6 week estimate. Measured against the
tree, the boot path does not exist in any form:

| Required | State at HEAD |
|----------|---------------|
| A bootable binary target | `rust/` has **no `[[bin]]`** — the only binary is `rust/sele4n-hal/src/bin/rw_lock_oracle.rs`, a host test oracle |
| Lean → aarch64 object code | `lakefile.toml` declares one **host** `[[lean_lib]]` and 70 **host** `[[lean_exe]]`s; no `precompileModules`, no `moreLinkArgs`, no cross-compilation rule |
| `libsele4n.a` for aarch64 | Nothing in the tree produces it, though `rust/sele4n-hal/src/boot.rs (rust_boot_main)` asserts it is linked |
| Bare-metal Lean runtime hosting | Zero hits for `lean_initialize_runtime_module` / `lean_io_mark_end_initialization` anywhere |
| `@[export] lean_kernel_main` | **Absent.** `rust/sele4n-hal/src/boot.rs (rust_boot_main)` declares it `extern "C"`; the only exported kernel entry is `lean_secondary_kernel_main` (`SeLe4n/Kernel/SecondaryEntry.lean`) — and that module is *staged*, outside the production closure |
| aarch64 compile coverage | **None** in tree or CI: 67 cfg-gated blocks, 59 `asm!` sites and all three `.S` files are never compiled |

The estimate and the phase-goal sentence both need rewriting before SM10
is planned against them. This is the largest single risk in the register.

### 2.3 SM9 is missing from the SM10 plan

`SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` predates SM9 and never absorbed it:
`grep -c DECLASSIFICATION` returns **0**. The §5 theorem tally reads
"16 SM0 + 1 SM1 + 22 SM2 + 28 SM3 + ~50 SM4 + 30 SM5 + 25 SM6 + 14 SM7 +
18 SM8 + 5 SM10 = 209 ≈ 210" — SM8 to SM10 with **no SM9 term** — and the
archive list names 11 plan files, omitting
`SMP_DECLASSIFICATION_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`. SM10.3.13 ("verify all 210 SM
theorems land at HEAD") and the `wsm_theorem_count` marker theorem would
both certify a number computed as if SM9 never happened, though SM9 is a
landed phase (CLOSED v0.33.100) whose surface carries hundreds of theorems.

Derive `wsm_theorem_count` from a generated manifest rather than a
hand-summed literal, so the marker cannot again certify a stale number.

### 2.4 Method and how to read this register

19 plans and 6 cross-cutting sweeps (gates, debt register, doc
consistency, boot path, implement-the-improvement, security) were each
audited by an independent agent working from the tree, and **every**
resulting finding was then re-checked by a separate adversarial verifier
instructed to refute it. 250 agents; 171 findings confirmed, 53 refuted.

Two conventions govern this document:

- **Severity is calibrated to SM10**, not to the abstract seriousness of
 the defect. `blocksSm10` means the finding blocks *starting* SM10 —
 work SM10 is itself assigned is not a blocker.
- **The implement-the-improvement rule applies to every remediation.**
 Where documentation describes something better than the code, the fix
 is to implement the improvement; no finding here is remediated by
 weakening a claim. (CLAUDE.md, "Implement-the-improvement rule".)

Findings that name a symbol were checked through
`scripts/lean_code_view.py --overlay`, the comment-free code view, so a
name surviving only in a docstring does not count as landed.

## 3. The three blockers

Each blocks *starting* SM10, as distinct from work SM10 is itself supposed to do.

**Status at `v0.34.42`: all three are closed**, each by the WS-RR phase that
owns it — blocker 1 by RR0 at `v0.34.26` (the de-threading workstream is
registered as **WS-DT** in `docs/REGISTERED_DEBT.md`, in
`SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` §2 Dependencies, and in CLAUDE.md/AGENTS.md's
standing-constraints block, with RR3 as its closure target), and blockers 2 and
3 by RR2 at `v0.34.42`.  RR3 then closed **WS-DT itself** at `v0.34.43` —
de-threading and dispatch payoff both — so blocker 1's underlying debt is
done, not merely registered.  The closure narratives are appended to each finding
below; the findings themselves are left as the audit wrote them, since this
register is a record of what was found rather than a status board.

#### 1. An open, materially incomplete workstream registered in no durable index — SM10 would close WS-SM with it silently dropped

- **Severity**: critical · **Kind**: `unregistered-debt` · **Blocks SM10 start**: yes
- **Source plan**: `docs/planning/IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md (Status table: D1, D6, D8 all 🔄 with named remaining work)
- **Tree cite**: docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md and (dependency list omits it); CLAUDE.md "Standing constraints and registered debt" (no entry)

SM10.2.4 (README capability claim) and SM10.2.6 (CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX entries) are the sub-tasks that would write v1.0.0 verification claims over this surface.

REGISTRATION ABSENCE VERIFIED. `grep -c IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING` = 0 in CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md, docs/REGISTERED_DEBT.md, docs/CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md, docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md, docs/planning/SMP_MULTICORE_COMPLETION_PLAN.md, README.md, docs/spec/SELE4N_SPEC.md, scripts/website_link_manifest.txt. Only CHANGELOG.md carries references (94, all historical `Refs:` lines). Searching the concept under other wording (de-thread/dethread/threaded post-state) in those index docs also returns nothing. ALL REFUTATION ANGLES FAILED. 1. Landed later? No. Newest de-threading CHANGELOG entry is CHANGELOG.md (v0.32.51, "D8 Slice 6"); tree is v0.34.1 — stalled ~1.5 minor versions. 2. Deliberate post-v1.0.0 deferral? No. `grep -niE "v1\.0\.0|post-release|post-v1|deferred|out of scope"` on IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md returns ZERO hits. Contrast docs/planning/SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md, which self-declares "(p…

**Remediation.** Before SM10.2.6/A.4 write any claim: add a row for this workstream to `docs/REGISTERED_DEBT.md` (the project's declared single canonical source for workstream status) with its real state (D0/D2/D2′/D3/D4/D5/D7 closed; D1, D6, D8 open), add it to `SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` §2 Dependencies, and add a bullet to CLAUDE.md's "Standing constraints and registered debt" naming the two still-threaded conjuncts so new code does not assume `ipcInvariantFull` is end-to-end machine-checked. Give each open slice an explicit closure target version.

#### 2. Cross-core SchedContext donation never migrates the CBS replenish queue, breaking the SM5.H affinity invariant on a live path

- **Severity**: high · **Kind**: `unclosed-work` · **Blocks SM10 start**: yes
- **Source plan**: `docs/planning/SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md section 4.3 there; sub-task rows SM6.A.5 and SM6.C.3
- **Tree cite**: SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Operations/Endpoint.lean (donateSchedContext) and SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointReplyDispatch.lean (applyReplyDonationOnCore) (no migration); contrast SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/Cancellation.lean (cancelDonatedDonationOnCore) (migration present)

**Observed.** Plan section 4.3 states: "if the receiver inherits the SC and is on a different core, the SC's CBS replenish queue migrates per SM5.H.4." No donation path does this. `donateSchedContext` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Operations/Endpoint.lean (donateSchedContext)) sets `sc.boundThread := some serverTid` and performs three storeObjects only; its own `donateSchedContext_scheduler_eq` proves the scheduler is untouched. `applyCallDonation` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Operations/Donation/Primitives.lean (applyCallDonation)) has no OnCore variant at all. `applyReplyDonationOnCore` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointReplyDispatch.lean (applyReplyDonationOnCore)) calls `returnDonatedSchedContextValid` — whose docstring calls it "an object-store-only rebinding that is cross-core-safe" — then only `removeRunnableOnCore`. `migrateSchedContextReplenishment` has exactly one live call site in the tree: SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/Cancellation.lean (cancelDonatedDonationOnCore) (the SM6.E donated arm), showing the migration is considered required on the return path. The invariant it protects, `replenishQueueAffinityConsistentOnCore` (SeLe4n/Kernel/Scheduler/Operations/PerCoreCbs.lean (replenishQueueAffinityConsistentOnCore)), requires `determineTargetCore st tid = c` for every SC in core c's replenish queue, and has preservation theorems only for tick/schedule…

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED. Every element checks out against the tree, and the strongest corroboration is the repo's own prose. 1. The plan claim is real and verbatim: docs/planning/SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md — "if the receiver inherits the SC and is on a different core, the SC's CBS replenish queue migrates per SM5.H.4." The risk table (there) repeats it: "Donation across cores leaks CBS budget | Migration under lock-set; SM5.H.4". 2. No donation path migrates. `applyCallDonation` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Operations/Donation/Primitives.lean (applyCallDonation)) takes no CoreId; the LIVE cross-core `.call` dispatch calls it unchanged at SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointCallDispatch.lean (endpointCallCrossCoreDispatch), and the docstring at (lockSet_endpointReply_donation_extension) asserts it is "an object-store-only update that is cross-core-safe". `applyReplyDonationOnCore` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointReplyDispatch.lean (applyReplyDonationOnCore)) is `returnDonatedSchedContextValid` + `removeRunnableOnCore` only; its caller `endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatch` ( (endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatch)) adds PIP reversio…

**Remediation.** Implement the migration the plan describes: add `applyCallDonationOnCore` threading donor and donee home cores and calling `migrateSchedContextReplenishment st scId donorHome doneeHome`, and add the mirror call inside `applyReplyDonationOnCore` (replier home to original-owner home), exactly as `cancelDonatedDonationOnCore` already does. Then prove `applyCallDonationOnCore`/`applyReplyDonationOnCore_preserves_replenishQueueAffinityConsistentOnCore` and extend both donation lock-sets with the two `SchedLockId.replenishQueue` write locks (`migrateSchedContextReplenishmentLockSet`). Do not weaken section 4.3.

**CLOSED at `v0.34.42` (WS-RR RR2), and wider than reported.**  There are
**three** live donation paths, not two: `.replyRecv` performs a *pair* of
hand-offs — the recorded server returns its donated context to the client it was
serving, then, when the receive rendezvoused with a queued `Call`, the next
caller's context is immediately donated to the receiver — and neither migrated.
This finding did not name it (`replyRecvReturnDonation`,
`SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean`), and neither did the independent verification, because
both enumerated the donation *primitives* and this path calls them from the API
layer.  On a delegated reply cap the receiver need not be the recorded server, so
one `.replyRecv` could leave a SchedContext's replenishments two cores from the
thread now bound to it.  It was found during RR2's own closure review and closed
in the same cut as RR2.20 — the enumeration-versus-derivation shape CLAUDE.md's
key-conventions section warns about, on a finding rather than on a gate.

All three now migrate, and the migration is proved rather than asserted.
`applyCallDonationOnCore`
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Operations/Donation.lean`, RR2.1) threads the donor and
donee home cores and calls `migrateSchedContextReplenishment`;
`applyReplyDonationOnCore` (`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointReplyDispatch.lean`,
RR2.8) does the mirror hop, replier home to original-owner home.  Each carries
`_preserves_replenishQueueAffinityConsistent_smp` (RR2.3, RR2.9), so the SM5.H
invariant is *restored by* the transition rather than assumed of its post-state,
and each carries the frame lemmas (`_objects_eq`, `_machine_eq`,
`_runQueue_current_eq`) that let callers see the migration is the only
scheduler-visible effect.  Both lock-sets were widened to the two
`SchedLockId.replenishQueue` writes the migration performs
(`applyCallDonationOnCoreSchedLockSet_covers_migration`,
`applyReplyDonationOnCoreSchedLockSet_covers_migration`, RR2.4 and RR2.10) and
both remain `pairwise_le` under the cross-domain order and inside
`maxLockSetSize`; the two dispatch-level lock-sets that contain them
(`endpointCallCrossCoreDispatchSchedLockSet`,
`endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatchSchedLockSet`) carry `_covers_donation` so the
widening cannot be silently dropped from the enclosing set.  A same-core
donation is a no-op by `migrateSchedContextReplenishment_noop`, so the common
case costs nothing.  Section 4.3 was not weakened.

The third path carries the same guarantee
(`replyRecvReturnDonation_preserves_replenishQueueAffinityConsistent_smp`), built
from the shared return-plus-migration lemma RR2.9's theorem was factored into,
RR2.3's call-path theorem, and a frame the priority-inheritance chain walk had
never been given — `propagatePipChainCrossCore_replenish_readings`, which shows
the walk leaves all three readings the invariant makes untouched.  SM8.B's
write set for that path was updated to branch on the migrated states in the same
cut; neither migration adds a core to it.  RR2.19 gives the property
executable coverage: `tests/SmpIpcSuite.lean` §3.9b runs a 4-core fixture in
which the entry leaves the donor's replenish queue and appears on the donee's,
and asserts the same-core case moves nothing.

#### 3. Live `.send` arm has no ipcInvariantFull preservation, and the closure target SM8 names does not carry it while SM6.D claims coverage

- **Source plan**: `docs/planning/SMP_INFORMATION_FLOW_PLAN.md`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_INFORMATION_FLOW_PLAN.md (§5 SM8.B, 'Registered debt (b) -- The live `.send` sits outside the invariant-preservation chain -- closure target SM6.D's open bundle-carriage list')
- **Tree cite**: SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSend.lean (endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore) (endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore, no preservation theorem); SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchWithCap) and (the live arms); docs/planning/SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md

**Observed.** SM8's registered debt (b) is accurate: `SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSend.lean` contains zero occurrences of `preserves_ipcInvariantFull` (grep count 0), while the call-side sibling `EndpointCallInvariant.lean` has seven. `endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSend.lean (endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore)) is what the live dispatch calls -- `SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchWithCap)` (dispatchWithCap) and `endpointSendCrossCoreDispatchChecked` at `SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchWithCapChecked)` (dispatchWithCapChecked). But the closure target the plan names does not exist: `docs/planning/SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md` §SM6.D 'Remaining tracked debt (recorded, not silent)' ((notificationSignal_passiveServerIdleFrameOnCore)) lists exactly three items -- bound-notification delivery, `withLockSet` bundle preservation, and completeness sugar -- none of them the send. Worse, the paragraph immediately above it ((notificationSignal_passiveServerIdleFrameOnCore)) affirmatively claims 'The capability-carrying trio behind the live `.send` dispatch is likewise covered', citing `endpointSendDualWithCaps_preserves_ipcInvariantFull_perCore` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Invariant/PerCoreBundlePreservation.lean (endpointSendDualWithCaps_preserves_ipcInvariantFull_perCore)) -- a theorem about the *single-core, boot-pinned* function that stopped being the live arm when PR #861 round 12 re-routed `.send` at v0.33.5. `docs/planning/IPC…

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED, and the blast radius is larger than the auditor reported. **1. The gap is real and has no alternative home.** `SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSend.lean` has 0 occurrences of `preserves_ipcInvariantFull`. I enumerated every `endpointSend*OnCore*` identifier in the tree (14 total): the only theorems are `_absent_endpoint`, `_bootCore_block_eq_single` ( (endpointSendDualOnCore_bootCore_block_eq_single)), `_bootCore_rendezvous_eq_single` ( (endpointSendDualOnCore_bootCore_rendezvous_eq_single)), `_tooLarge` ( (endpointSendDualOnCore_tooLarge)), `_tooManyCaps` ( (endpointSendDualOnCore_tooManyCaps)), `_no_caps` ( (endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore_no_caps)), `_eq_single_on_bootCore`, plus five NI theorems in `SeLe4n/Kernel/InformationFlow/NonInterferenceCrossCore.lean (endpointSendDualOnCore_confinedToCores)`. No re-export hub, no submodule, no rename. I also checked for weaker coverage: a grep for any `endpointSendDual.*OnCore.*preserves` across all of `SeLe4n/` returns **empty** — not even the non-`Full` `preserves_ipcInvariant`. And there is no dispatch-level substitute: `endpointSendCrossCoreDispatchChecked` has only `_flow_denied`/`_flow_allowed` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSend.lean (endpointSendCrossCoreDispatchChecked_flow_denied),436) and two NI theorems. **2. The asymmetry is structur…

**Remediation.** Implement the theorems, per the implement-the-improvement rule -- do not soften either plan. The cheap first slice is already staged: `endpointSendDualOnCore_bootCore_block_eq_single` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSend.lean (endpointSendDualOnCore_bootCore_block_eq_single)) and `endpointSendDualOnCore_bootCore_rendezvous_eq_single` ( (storeTcbIpcStateAndMessage_passiveServerIdleFrameOnCore)) rewrite both success arms to the single-core transition, so the boot-core instance of `endpointSendDualOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariantFull{,_perCore}` reduces to the existing `endpointSendDual_preserves_ipcInvariantFull_perCore` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Invariant/PerCoreBundlePreservation.lean (endpointSendDual_preserves_ipcInvariantFull_perCore)); the general per-core case follows the `endpointReceiveDualOnCore_post_agrees` LookupCongruence pattern, and the WithCaps form composes `ipcUnwrapCaps_passiveServerIdleFrameOnCore` as its siblings do. In the same PR, correct SM6.D's scope note to name `endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore` rather than the retired single-core function, and add the item to SM6.D's t…

**CLOSED at `v0.34.42` (WS-RR RR2).**  The live arm carries the bundle, and it
is about the function the live arm actually calls.
`endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` and
`_perCore` (`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSendInvariant.lean`, RR2.14 and
RR2.15) sit over `endpointSendDualOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariantFull{,_perCore}`,
which the same cut built for the inner transition the verification pass had also
missed.  The finding's "cheap first slice" turned out not to be the one taken:
rather than reduce to the boot-core instance, the per-core case is proved
directly from the transition's read agreement, so the theorem says something
about core 3 as well as core 0.  `clearWokenReceiverStash` — the fifth live arm,
which the finding's §5 sibling named — got its own bundle in the same cut
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean`, RR2.16).  SM6.D's scope note was corrected to name
`endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore` rather than the retired single-core function.

One thing the cut also removed rather than added: the WithCaps composition needs
`ipcUnwrapCaps_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`, which previously took its dual-queue
and badge conjuncts as *post-state* hypotheses.  RR2.15 retyped it to take the
receiver's CNode and the capability list instead and discharged both from
lemmas already in the tree, so the bundle establishes them rather than assuming
them — two of the eight threaded sites RR3.11 is scheduled to close, closed
early because the send bundle could not honestly compose over them.

## 4. Security and soundness findings

#### 1. VM faults are unhandled: data/instruction abort returns to the faulting instruction, producing an unrecoverable fault loop, registered only as a source comment

- **Severity**: high · **Kind**: `unregistered-debt` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `security`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md ('SM10 — Documentation, Tests, Version Closure') and §3, whose sub-tasks SM10.2-E cover docs sync, test suites, version bump, inventory closure and QEMU validation only; CLAUDE.md 'Standing constraints and registered debt', which does not list fault handl…
- **Tree cite**: rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.rs (esr_ec) (the placeholder comment) and the DABT/IABT arms at trap.rs; rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.S (ELR restored unchanged)

**Observed.** In `handle_synchronous_exception` (rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.rs (handle_synchronous_exception)), the DABT_LOWER/DABT_CURRENT and IABT_LOWER/IABT_CURRENT arms call `record_vm_fault` (a Relaxed counter increment, rust/sele4n-hal/src/per_cpu_stats.rs (record_vm_fault)) and then `frame.set_x0(error_code::VM_FAULT)` - nothing else. No fault IPC is delivered, no thread is suspended, and grep for `.elr =`, `set_elr` and `advance_pc` across rust/sele4n-hal/src/*.rs returns zero hits, so `elr_el1` (TrapFrame offset 256) is restored unchanged by rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.S before `eret`. For a data abort ELR points at the faulting instruction, so the thread re-executes it and faults again indefinitely. VBAR_EL1 is genuinely installed at boot (rust/sele4n-hal/src/boot.rs (rust_boot_main)), so this is the live path once threads run. The only record of this gap anywhere is the parenthetical '/// - Data/Instruction Abort: VM fault handling (placeholder)' at trap.rs; grep finds no mention of vmFault, fault handler, or fault IPC in docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md or in CLAUDE.md's 'Standing constraints and registered debt' section.

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED — every mechanical claim reproduces against the tree, and I could not find the missing artifact under any other name, module, hub, or later version. **1. The handler arms do exactly what the finding says.** `rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.rs (handle_synchronous_exception)` (DABT_LOWER|DABT_CURRENT) and (IABT_LOWER|IABT_CURRENT) contain only `crate::per_cpu_stats::record_vm_fault;` + `frame.set_x0(error_code::VM_FAULT);`. `record_vm_fault` is a `fetch_add(1, Ordering::Relaxed)` on a per-CPU counter (`rust/sele4n-hal/src/per_cpu_stats.rs (record_vm_fault)`) — a statistic, not a control-flow action. No suspend, no reschedule, no fault delivery. **2. ELR is genuinely restored unchanged.** `trap.S`'s `restore_context` does `ldr x0,[sp,#264]; msr spsr_el1,x0` then `ldr x0,[sp,#256]; msr elr_el1,x0` (rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.S) and `eret` (rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.S). `TrapFrame.elr_el1` is at offset 256 (`trap.rs`, asserted at `trap.rs`). The only mutators the frame exposes are `set_x0..set_x5` / `set_return_frame` (`trap.rs`) — there is no…

**Remediation.** Implement fault handling rather than documenting the placeholder. At minimum the abort arms must take the faulting thread out of the run queue - suspend it and deliver a fault message to its registered fault handler, matching seL4's fault-IPC model - so that a faulting thread cannot livelock its core. Per CLAUDE.md's rule that deferred items must never live in source comments, lift this into the debt register (docs/audits/ or docs/REGISTERED_DEBT.md) with an explicit closure target in the same PR, and either widen SM10's scope to include it or add a preceding phase; a kernel declared bootable at v1.0.0 in which any unprivileged unmapped-memory access wedges a core is not release-ready.

#### 2. "Cancellation non-interference - LANDED" rests on an unproven hypothesis whose closure form returns its own premise

- **Severity**: medium · **Kind**: `soundness` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `docs/planning/SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_CROSS_CORE_IPC_PLAN.md ("Cancellation non-interference - LANDED") with the qualifying sentence there
- **Tree cite**: SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/CancellationNI.lean (cancelIpcBlockingOnCore_cancellation_NI) (hTeardownProj hypothesis) and SeLe4n/Kernel/InformationFlow/Invariant/Operations.lean (suspendThread_preserves_projection) (body is `hProjEq st' hStep`)

**Observed.** Plan section SM6.E completion cut item 2 is headed "Cancellation non-interference - LANDED (staged CancellationNI). Every SM6.E-new state effect is discharged substantively". The blocked-arm composites are not: `cancelIpcBlockingOnCore_cancellation_NI` (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/CancellationNI.lean (cancelIpcBlockingOnCore_cancellation_NI)) and `_smp` ( (cdt_only_preserves_projection')) both take `hTeardownProj : projectState ctx observer (cancelIpcBlocking st victim tcb) = projectState ctx observer st` as a hypothesis, discharge only the deschedule half via removeRunnableOnCore_preserves_projection, and close with `exact hTeardownProj`. The plan says that obligation is "the same obligation the production closure form (suspendThread_preserves_projection G3, AK6-F.18) has always documented". That production form, at SeLe4n/Kernel/InformationFlow/Invariant/Operations.lean (suspendThread_preserves_projection), has body `hProjEq st' hStep` — it takes its own conclusion as an argument and hands it back, so it proves nothing. Its docstring names the real obstruction: spliceOutMidQueueNode rewrites the victim's queue-neighbour TCBs' queuePrev/queueNext, queue-link fields survive projection, and discharging it needs the dual-queue endpoint-label invariant the codebase has not built.…

**Independent verification.** Every factual cite in the finding checks out, but the severity/kind/blocker framing does not survive the tree. VERIFIED TRUE: - SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/CancellationNI.lean (cancelIpcBlockingOnCore_cancellation_NI) and (capTargetObservable) take `hTeardownProj : projectState ctx observer (cancelIpcBlocking st victim tcb) = projectState ctx observer st` and close with `exact hTeardownProj` (bodies end at (projectServiceRegistry_consistent_with_presence) and (capTargetObservable)). Only the deschedule half is substantive (removeRunnableOnCore_preserves_projection{,OnCore}). - SeLe4n/Kernel/InformationFlow/Invariant/Operations.lean (suspendThread_preserves_projection) `suspendThread_preserves_projection` has body `hProjEq st' hStep` (there) — it takes its own conclusion and returns it. - The obstruction is real, not rhetorical: `spliceOutMidQueueNode` (SeLe4n/Kernel/Lifecycle/Operations/Cleanup.lean (spliceOutMidQueueNode)) rewrites neighbour TCBs' queueNext/queuePrev, and `projectKernelObject` (SeLe4n/Kernel/InformationFlow/Projection.lean (projectKernelObject)) strips registerContext/schedContextBinding/pipBoost/pendingMessage/timedOut/cpuAffinity/replyObject/pendingReceiveReply/lock b…

**Remediation.** Build the missing foundation rather than restating the claim: add the dual-queue endpoint-label invariant (threads queued on an endpoint share its label), prove `spliceOutMidQueueNode_preserves_projection{,OnCore}` under it, compose into a substantive `cancelIpcBlocking_preserves_projection`, and replace the closure form `suspendThread_preserves_projection` with the real theorem so hTeardownProj discharges. The same invariant also closes the bound-delivery NI debt below, making this the highest-leverage remaining SM6 item.

**PARTIALLY CLOSED at `v0.34.42` (WS-RR RR2.18); residual carried to RR3.**  The
remediation's shape is right but its scope is one invariant wider than the
finding needs, and RR2 closed the part that does not need it.  `cancelIpcBlocking`
branches on the victim's `ipcState`, and only three of its five arms splice a
queue:

- `.ready` — and every other non-blocking state — leaves `st` unchanged, so its
  projection equality is `rfl`.  That arm was always in reach; RR2.18 states it
  (`cancelIpcBlockingOnCore_ready_cancellation_NI{,_smp}`).
- `.blockedOnReply` is now discharged **substantively**.
  `cancelIpcBlocking_blockedOnReply_preserves_projection`
  (`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/CancellationNI.lean` §5) is composed from four
  new frames — `restoreToReady_preserves_projection_high`,
  `clearTcbReplyObject_preserves_projection_high`,
  `clearReplyObjectCaller_preserves_projection` and
  `consumeReplyLink_preserves_projection_high` — and feeds
  `cancelIpcBlockingOnCore_reply_cancellation_NI`, which takes **no**
  `hTeardownProj`.  This arm reaches no queue: it walks the Reply object's
  caller link, which is precisely why it does not need the missing invariant.
- the three queue arms (`.blockedOnSend`, `.blockedOnReceive`,
  `.waitingOnNotification`) still take `hTeardownProj`.  The obstruction the
  finding names is real and unmoved: `spliceOutMidQueueNode` rewrites the
  neighbour TCBs' `queueNext`/`queuePrev`, those fields survive `projectState`,
  and making the rewrite invisible needs the endpoint/notification queue
  label-uniformity invariant — a low endpoint holding a high waiter would make
  the cancellation genuinely observable, so the invariant is not a formality.

Stating that invariant is a lemma; *establishing* it is a workstream.  It has to
hold at every enqueue site and be preserved by every transition that touches a
queue, which is a wider surface than RR2's live-path remit, so RR2 is recorded
as landing with the clause open rather than as closing it.  Closure target
**RR3**.  The production closure form `suspendThread_preserves_projection` is
unchanged and still returns its own premise; replacing it belongs to the same
RR3 slice, since it is the same obligation stated one level up.

#### 3. Platform/FFI.lean asserts an unqualified boot identity-map that the boot page tables do not provide above 3 GiB

- **Status**: **CLOSED at v0.34.57** (WS-RR RR7.2, with RR7.1).  The tables were extended, not the claim qualified: `mmu::boot_mapping_for` maps every RAM frame the board reports and `cache::apply_icache_invalidation` (plus the sibling `cache_clean_pagetable_range` seam) refuses an operand outside that window at the extent it maintains.
- **Severity**: medium · **Kind**: `soundness` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `docs/planning/SMP_TLB_SHOOTDOWN_PLAN.md`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_TLB_SHOOTDOWN_PLAN.md §"SM7.D deferred items" row 1 ("live the moment the image boots on a 4 GB or 8 GB Pi 5"; "Must cover the whole operand family, not just.unifyPage")
- **Tree cite**: SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (ffiIcMaintenance) vs rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (build_identity_tables); SeLe4n/Platform/RPi5/Board.lean (rpi5MachineConfig)

**Observed.** SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (ffiIcMaintenance) states without qualification: "the boot tables identity-map RAM, so a RAM frame's kernel VA equals its PA and `ICacheInvalidation.toPaddr` is the correct operand", and (ffiIcMaintenance) repeats it for.unifyPage ("the same operand under the same identity-map argument"). The boot tables do not support that claim. rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (build_identity_tables) (build_identity_tables) populates exactly four L1 block descriptors: entries 0..2 = 0x0000_0000–0xBFFF_FFFF as BLOCK_NORMAL (Normal WB cacheable), and entry 3 = 0xC000_0000–0xFFFF_FFFF as BLOCK_DEVICE (Device-nGnRnE, PXN|UXN, not Inner Shareable — rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (block_device_has_pxn_and_uxn) asserts SH_INNER is clear). Nothing above 4 GiB is mapped at all, while SeLe4n/Platform/RPi5/Board.lean (rpi5MachineConfig) sets physicalAddressWidth := 44. So on a 4 GB or 8 GB Pi 5 a DC CVAU / IC IVAU / DC CVAU-range issued against a frame above 3 GiB either faults at EL1 or operates through a Device alias — a silent under-maintenance, which is exactly the SMP-C4 hazard SM7 exists to close, arriving from the cache side. The SM7 plan's deferred item 1 states this correctly and defers it to SM10.1; the code site carries none of that caveat, so a reader of FFI.lean is told the o…

**Independent verification.** TECHNICAL SUBSTANCE: CONFIRMED. Every cited fact verified verbatim, and one independent check strengthens it. - SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (ffiIcMaintenance) reads exactly "the boot tables identity-map RAM, so a RAM frame's kernel VA equals its PA and `ICacheInvalidation.toPaddr` is the correct operand"; repeats it for `.unifyPage` ("the same operand under the same identity-map argument"). No qualifier anywhere in the file (grep for Device/3 GiB/0xC000/kernel VA over FFI.lean: zero hits in that docstring's scope). - rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (build_identity_tables) populates exactly four L1 block descriptors: entries 0..2 = `addr | BLOCK_NORMAL` (0x0–0xBFFF_FFFF), entry 3 = `(3u64 << 30) | BLOCK_DEVICE`. Nothing above 4 GiB. rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (BLOCK_NORMAL)/54 confirm BLOCK_NORMAL carries SH_INNER + ATTR_IDX_NORMAL while BLOCK_DEVICE carries PXN|UXN|ATTR_IDX_DEVICE and no SH_INNER; rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (block_device_has_pxn_and_uxn) asserts that (`assert_eq!(BLOCK_DEVICE & SH_INNER, 0)`). - SeLe4n/Platform/RPi5/Board.lean (rpi5MachineConfig) `physicalAddressWidth := 44`. - No PA→ker…

**Remediation.** Implement the improvement the deferred item already specifies: add a PA→kernel-VA translation for the whole ICacheInvalidation operand family (iallu / ivauPage / unifyPage / cleanRangeIallu) with a fail-closed reject for frames outside the cacheable window, and pin the window against mmu.rs's L1 population so the two cannot drift. If the full translation cannot land in one cut, the minimal closure is the fail-closed reject on its own: refusing operands outside the cacheable window makes the docstring's identity-map claim true within an enforced bound, which is the implement-the-improvement direction. Qualifying the docstring to describe the narrower behaviour is NOT an acceptable interim step — the project forbids rewriting a description to match inferior code, and where the implementation is genuinely out of scope the correct outcome is to defer the release as tracked debt, never to ship a documentation-only patch.

#### 4. The boot MMU maps 960 MiB that link.ld declares as RAM as Device memory, and maps nothing above 4 GiB

- **Status**: **CLOSED at v0.34.57** (WS-RR RR7.1).  `build_identity_tables` now derives every descriptor from `mmu::boot_mapping_for`, so `0xC000_0000`–`0xFBFF_FFFF` is Normal RAM, the firmware carve-out and the reserved tail are unmapped, and RAM above 4 GiB is mapped from the device tree's `/memory` node.  `scripts/check_physical_address_width.sh` holds the window equal across `mmu.rs`, `Board.lean` and `link.ld`.  **The same cut fixed a defect this finding did not name**: the table `TTBR0_EL1` pointed at held level-1 block descriptors while `TCR_EL1.T0SZ = 16` makes level 0 the initial lookup level, so every walk would have taken a translation fault at MMU enable — the boot map is now a real L0 → L1 → L2 structure.
- **Severity**: medium · **Kind**: `soundness` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `bootpath`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md §3 SM10.1 (the phase that will place a kernel heap)
- **Tree cite**: rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (compute_sctlr_el1_bitmap),344-357 vs rust/sele4n-hal/link.ld

**Observed.** rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (build_identity_tables) populates exactly four L1 block entries: 0x0-0xBFFFFFFF Normal cacheable, 0xC0000000-0xFFFFFFFF Device. rust/sele4n-hal/link.ld declares `RAM (rwx) : ORIGIN = 0x80000, LENGTH = 0xFBF80000` - i.e. usable RAM up to 0xFC000000. The 0xC0000000-0xFC000000 span (960 MiB) is therefore linker-declared RAM mapped as Device: no cacheability, no unaligned access, no speculation. rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (compute_sctlr_el1_bitmap) confirms only entries 0..3 are populated, so on 8/16 GiB RPi 5 boards all RAM above 4 GiB is unmapped. Today nothing allocates there (the image and both stack regions sit near 0x80000), which is why this has stayed latent - but the kernel heap SM10.1 must add for the Lean runtime is exactly the first consumer that would.

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED. Every cited fact holds, and the tree gives it more support than the auditor claimed. Primary facts verified: - `rust/sele4n-hal/link.ld` — `RAM (rwx) : ORIGIN = 0x80000, LENGTH = 0xFBF80000`. 0x80000 + 0xFBF80000 = 0xFC000000 exactly (checked). The file's own header, `rust/sele4n-hal/link.ld`, states "0x00000000 - 0xFC000000 : RAM (4032 MiB, BCM2712 4GB model)". - `rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (build_identity_tables)` — `build_identity_tables` populates entries 0..2 as `BLOCK_NORMAL` and `table.entries[3] = (3u64 << 30) | BLOCK_DEVICE`, i.e. 0xC0000000-0xFFFFFFFF Device. `rust/sele4n-hal/src/mmu.rs (compute_sctlr_el1_bitmap)` confirms "We only populate entries 0..3 (first 4 GiB)". The 0xC0000000-0xFC000000 overlap is 0x3C000000 = 960 MiB, as claimed. - Latency confirmed: `rust/sele4n-hal/link.ld` places `.stack` (64 KiB) and `.smp_stacks` (3x64 KiB) contiguously after `.bss`, all near 0x80000. Nothing lands in the affected span today. - No heap exists: zero `#[global_allocator]` / `GlobalAlloc` anywhere under `rust/` (grep clean), and `rust/sele4n-…

**Remediation.** Reconcile the two before SM10.1 places any heap: either narrow link.ld's RAM LENGTH to 0xBFF80000 so the linker cannot hand out Device-typed addresses, or extend build_identity_tables to map RAM up to the true peripheral base (0xFE000000) as Normal with a finer split for the 0xFE000000+ device window, and populate L1 entries beyond 3 for larger boards. Add the resulting bound to check_physical_address_width.sh's remit so it cannot drift again.

#### 5. The flagship 'syscall entry implies capability held' theorem covers only the legacy dispatch path, not the live checked path the hardware invokes

- **Status**: **CLOSED at v0.34.57** (WS-RR RR7.3).  `dispatchSyscallChecked_requires_right` and `syscallEntryChecked_implies_capability_held` state the guarantee on the live path over the *executing* core, through both gate shapes; `…_of_pre_state` restates it on the trapped-in state (via `resolveCapAddress_congr_objects`), and `Platform.FFI.syscallDispatchFromAbi_implies_capability_held` carries it to the exported seam.  The GitBook and `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md` citations name the live-path theorems.

- **Source plan**: `security`
- **Plan cite**: docs/CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md (WS-J1-C row: 'soundness theorems prove decode success and capability authorization are prerequisites for successful entry'); docs/gitbook/03-architecture-and-module-map.md and docs/gitbook/05-specification-and-roadmap.md, which cite `syscallEntry_implie…
- **Tree cite**: SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallEntry_implies_capability_held) and (theorems over the legacy path); SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchSyscallChecked) and (the live checked path, uncovered); SeLe4n/Kernel/SyscallDispatchEntry.lean (completeShootdownRounds_nil) (the live export)

**Observed.** `syscallEntry_implies_capability_held` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallEntry_implies_capability_held)) and `dispatchSyscall_requires_right` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchSyscall_requires_right)) are stated over `syscallEntry` / `dispatchSyscall`. But the live path is `@[export lean_syscall_dispatch_cross_core]` -> `syscallDispatchCrossCoreEntry` (SeLe4n/Kernel/SyscallDispatchEntry.lean (completeShootdownRounds_nil)) -> `Platform.FFI.syscallDispatchFromAbi` (SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (syscallDispatchFromAbi)) -> `syscallEntryChecked` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallEntryChecked)) -> `dispatchSyscallChecked` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchSyscallChecked)), confirmed by the Rust doc comment at rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (blocked_resume_sentinel_regs). Grepping theorem names over the code-view overlay yields only `dispatchSyscallChecked_applies_taint_plan`, `_preserves_projection`, `_audit_target_first` and `_audit_right_checked_second` - there is no general `dispatchSyscallChecked_requires_right`. Meanwhile the legacy `syscallEntry` is `bootCoreId`-pinned (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallEntry), `st.scheduler.currentOnCore bootCoreId`) and its only non-test callers are the trace harness and the exception model, so the theorem that the docs advertise as the syscall-entry soundness guarantee is proven about a path the hardware never takes. The code structurally does perform the check (via `syscallInvoke`), so this is a…

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED on facts, DOWNGRADED on severity and refuted on blocking. Verified true: (1) `dispatchSyscall_requires_right` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchSyscall_requires_right)) and `syscallEntry_implies_capability_held` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallEntry_implies_capability_held)) are stated over the legacy `syscallEntry`, which is `bootCoreId`-pinned (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallEntry)). (2) The live path is as claimed: `@[export lean_syscall_dispatch_cross_core]` at SeLe4n/Kernel/SyscallDispatchEntry.lean (completeShootdownRounds_nil) -> `Platform.FFI.syscallDispatchFromAbi` (SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (syscallDispatchFromAbi)) -> `syscallEntryChecked` (SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (syscallDispatchFromAbi)) -> `dispatchSyscallChecked` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchSyscallChecked)). (3) I rebuilt the comment-free overlay via `scripts/lean_code_view.py --overlay` and grepped every theorem matching requires_right|capability_held|check_rights over SeLe4n/ and tests/. Exactly five exist: SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallLookupCap_implies_capability_held), (syscallInvoke_requires_right), (resolveCapAddress_callers_check_rights), (dispatchSyscall_requires_right), (syscallEntry_implies_capability_held). There is no `dispatchSyscallChecked_requires_right` or `syscallEntryChecked_implies_capability_held` under any name, re-export hub, or sibling submodule. No later landing:…

**Remediation.** Prove the analogue on the live path - do not restate the claim more narrowly. Add `dispatchSyscallChecked_requires_right` and `syscallEntryChecked_implies_capability_held`, mirroring the existing proofs but parameterized over `executingCore` rather than `bootCoreId`, and handling the two `syscallChecksTargetFirst` arms via the existing `dispatchWithCapChecked_audit_insufficient_right_denied`. Then extend the chain through `syscallDispatchFromAbi` so the guarantee reaches the exported seam. Update the gitbook and CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX citations to name the live-path theorems once they exist. This belongs in SM10's theorem catalogue (§10) as a release-blocking item, since v1.0.0's headline claim is that every syscall is capability-gated.

#### 6. Fault-return arms use the retired v1 status convention (raw discriminant in x0, x1 untouched), inconsistent with SYSCALL_ABI_VERSION 2

- **Severity**: medium · **Kind**: `soundness` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `security`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SYSCALL_RETURN_ABI_PLAN.md §3.1/§3.6 (the six-register frame convention and `SYSCALL_ABI_VERSION = 2` pinned three ways); docs/CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md (WS-RA row: 'userspace `decode_response` decodes the label fail-closed')
- **Tree cite**: rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.rs (handle_synchronous_exception) (the four `set_x0`-only arms) and (from_u32) (error_code constants); contrast rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (error_frame_regs) (`error_frame_regs`)

**Observed.** Within the same handler, the SVC arm writes a proper six-register v2 frame via `frame.set_return_frame(...)`, where status rides the `MessageInfo` offset label in x1 and x0 carries the value. The DABT/IABT arms write `frame.set_x0(error_code::VM_FAULT)` (44) and the alignment/unknown arms write `frame.set_x0(error_code::USER_EXCEPTION)` (45) - all four leave x1 completely untouched (rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.rs (handle_synchronous_exception), error_code module at (decode_audit_field_too_large_error)). That is the retired ABI-v1 shape: a raw discriminant in the value register. Under v2, `decode_response` derives status solely from x1's 20-bit label, treating label 0 as success (rust/sele4n-abi/src/decode.rs; `decode_discriminant_zero_does_not_alias_success` pins that a zero label is a success). A resumed thread whose x1 happens to carry a label of 0 - x1 = 0 or any value under 512, which is common - therefore reads a fault return as a successful syscall carrying 44 or 45 as its badge/value. Today this is latent because the fault loop (previous finding) means the thread never coherently resumes, but the encoding is wrong now and will become observable the moment fault delivery is implemented.

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED against the tree; every refutation attempt failed. Factual core verified: - rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.rs (handle_synchronous_exception), 289, 297, 310 — the DABT/IABT/alignment/unknown arms each call `frame.set_x0(error_code::VM_FAULT)` (44) or `set_x0(error_code::USER_EXCEPTION)` (45) and write nothing else; x1 is left untouched. Constants at trap.rs. - Contrast in the same match: trap.rs, 272, 274 — the SVC arm writes all six registers via `set_return_frame` (SvcOutcome::Frame, `blocked_resume_sentinel_regs`, `error_frame_regs(disc)`). `error_frame_regs` at rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (error_frame_regs) = `[0, (disc+1)<<9, 0,0,0,0]`. - The v1 shape is explicitly retired: rust/sele4n-abi/src/decode.rs "The pre-WS-RA convention — `regs[0] != 0` *is* the error discriminant … is retired."; rust/sele4n-abi/src/decode.rs (decode_response) derives status solely from x1's 20-bit label, label 0 => Ok with regs[0] as badge/value. trap.rs (`set_x1` docstring) states x1's label carries the error status. - The project already adjudicated this…

**Remediation.** Make the fault paths speak ABI v2: replace each `frame.set_x0(error_code::X)` with `frame.set_return_frame(crate::svc_dispatch::error_frame_regs(disc))` for the corresponding KernelError discriminant (44 VmFault, 45 UserException), so no exception return can decode as success. Delete the now-unused `error_code` constants, or keep them only as the discriminants fed to `error_frame_regs`. Add a Rust test asserting that no exception-class arm leaves x1 unwritten, mirroring `error_frame_regs_offsets_every_discriminant`. Fold this into the same cut as the fault-delivery work, since both touch the same arms.

#### 7. The _atomic_under_lockSet family is a rfl instance of a body-agnostic lemma, so the atomicity half of two acceptance-gate items carries no operation-specific content

**Independent verification.** TECHNICAL SUBSTANCE: CONFIRMED (line numbers verified; two trivial offsets in the original). 1. blockBisim IS the theorem's own conclusion, one step at a time. SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/RwLockRefinement.lean (blockBisim) defines `blockBisim abs conc abs_op conc_block := rwLockSim (abs.applyOp abs_op) (concreteFoldBlock conc conc_block).toNat` (auditor cited (blockBisim); the `def` line is (blockBisim)). ListBlockBisim.cons requires it at (ListBlockBisim) (auditor cited (ListBlockBisim)). rust_rwLock_refines_lean ( (rust_rwLock_refines_lean)) takes `h_blocks_bisim : ListBlockBisim...` and concludes `rwLockSim (abs_ops.foldl RwLockState.applyOp initial_abs) (concreteFoldBlock initial_conc conc_blocks.flatten).toNat` — the n-step instance of the same predicate. The proof body ( (rust_rwLock_refines_lean)) is only List.foldl_cons / List.flatten_cons / List.foldl_append bookkeeping; h_sim_init is used only in the nil case. 2. opCorresponds plays no role. The corollary at (rust_rwLock_refines_lean_via_rustImplementsRwLock) binds `(_h_corresponds : rustImplementsRwLock conc_ops abs_ops)` at (rust_rwLock_refines_lean_via_rustImplementsRwLock); the proof ( (rust_rwLock_refines_lean_via_rustImplementsRwLock)…

**Remediation.** Close the gap in the direction the plan states, not by re-wording it: prove `opCorresponds`-chain + an explicit load-then-CAS trace-shape predicate ⇒ `ListBlockBisim`, so the 12 discharge lemmas compose into the main theorem without the caller supplying the conclusion. Until then, correct §5.4 there to agree with §1's PARTIAL (the §1 wording is the honest one) and state in §8's D-4 gate that the gate is unmet, with the `opCorresponds ⇒ ListBlockBisim` derivation named as the remaining obligation.

**CLOSED at `v0.34.50` (WS-RR RR6.15–RR6.19), in the direction the plan
states.**  The derivation exists: `honestBlock` is the load-then-CAS trace-shape
predicate as a *state-indexed* inductive — each constructor's CAS `expected` is
the value the block's own preceding load observed and its `new` is what
`rw_lock.rs` computes from it, so `tryRead_success c 999 999` is not a
`honestBlock` — and `listHonestBlocks_listBlockBisim` derives `ListBlockBisim`
from it by induction over the chain, discharged by the now-**total**
`honestBlock_blockBisim` family.  The two constructors with no discharge lemma
(`tryWrite_cas_retry`, `tryWrite_park_retry`) are covered, and the coverage is
derived from the constructor inventory rather than a hand-kept list, so a
constructor added later is a missing case rather than a silent gap.  The crux
was the promoting release: from `unheld`, `tryAcquireWrite c₀ · tryAcquireRead c₁
· releaseWrite c₀` leaves the abstract state with `readers = [c₁]` while the
concrete `fetch_and(READER_MASK)` is `0`, which is why the four original
discharges carried `_no_promote` / `_empty_queue` side conditions.  The block
contract was extended so a release block **carries the promoted waiters'
re-acquisition** (`casPromoteOps`, `casPromotePost`), and the promoting
discharges are proved over it — a contract change, not a lemma.
`rust_rwLock_refines_lean_honest` and
`rust_rwLock_refines_lean_via_rustImplementsRwLock_honest` therefore state the
result **without** the `ListBlockBisim` premise, and the corollary's
correspondence hypothesis is used rather than bound as `_h_corresponds`.

#### 9. suspend_thread_inner is still exported and commits kernel state outside the kernel-entry bracket

- **Severity**: low · **Kind**: `soundness` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `bootpath`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md §3 SM10.1 (which produces the linked image containing this symbol)
- **Tree cite**: SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (ffiSuspendThread),1770; rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs

**Observed.** SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (ffiSuspendThread) carries @[export suspend_thread_inner] on suspendThreadInner, whose success arm calls initialiseKernelState st' (SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (suspendThreadInner)) - a bare kernelStateRef.set with no with_kernel_entry bracket. rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs acknowledges it is unbracketed and asserts it is "not reachable from the trap path", which grep confirms for the HAL today. But @[export] makes it a live C symbol that will be present in the SM10.1 image, so any future Rust caller silently gets an unserialised kernel-state write.

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED. Every cited fact checks out and all refutation attempts failed. Verified in tree: - SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (ffiSuspendThread) carries `@[export suspend_thread_inner]` on `def suspendThreadInner`, exactly as claimed. - SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (suspendThreadInner) reads via `getKernelState`; SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (suspendThreadInner) commits via `initialiseKernelState st'`. SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (initialiseKernelState) shows `initialiseKernelState` is a bare `kernelStateRef.set`. So the body is an unguarded read-modify-write on the shared IO.Ref with no `with_kernel_entry` bracket. - rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs states verbatim that `syscall_dispatch_inner` and `suspend_thread_inner` "are not reachable from the trap path and are not bracketed." - The twin precedent is exact: scripts/test_tier3_invariant_surface.sh (_rg_shim_cleanup) is a `run_negative_check` pinning `@[export syscall_dispatch_inner]` dead (WS-RA). A later positive `run_check` still requires `@[export suspend_thread_inner]`, with neighbouring prose saying it "stays as the single-core entry." - SeLe4n.l…

**Remediation.** Retire the @[export] (WS-RA already removed its twin syscall_dispatch_inner, SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (icMaintenanceBroadcast_cleanRangeIallu_encoding)) keeping the Lean definition for the test suites, or route it through the same bracket as sele4n_suspend_thread. Do not leave a committing C symbol in the shipped image whose only protection is that nothing currently calls it.

#### 10. Two lock- and vector-safety tripwires are debug_assert! and vanish from the release image

- **Severity**: low · **Kind**: `soundness` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no

**Observed.** rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs (assert_not_holding_round_lock) implements assert_not_holding_round_lock with debug_assert!, documented at (cleanup) as the guard that makes a future lock-order cycle "fail loudly at the point of the mistake rather than as a hang". rust/sele4n-hal/src/boot.rs (__exception_vectors) checks VBAR_EL1's 2048-byte alignment with debug_assert_eq!, documented at as producing "a clean halt rather than the architectural UNDEFINED instruction the next exception would produce". Both compile out under --release, which is how a kernel8.img would be built (scripts/test_qemu.sh (cleanup) already uses cargo build --release).

**Independent verification.** I tried to refute this and could not — every cited fact holds, and the recommendation is the only remediation direction the project's own rules permit. VERIFIED AS CLAIMED: - rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs (assert_not_holding_round_lock) is `debug_assert!(!crate::shootdown::round_lock_is_held,...)`, and the docstring at (cleanup) does claim it makes a future lock-order mistake "fail loudly at the point of the mistake rather than as a hang." - rust/sele4n-hal/src/boot.rs (__exception_vectors) is `debug_assert_eq!(vbar % 2048, 0,...)`, documented at as making a regressed toolchain "surface as a clean halt rather than the architectural UNDEFINED instruction the next exception would produce." - The strip is real: rust/Cargo.toml defines `[profile.dev]` and `[profile.release]` with only `panic = "abort"`; neither sets `debug-assertions`, so both checks vanish under `--release`. scripts/test_qemu.sh (cleanup) already builds `cargo build --release --target aarch64-unknown-none -p sele4n-hal`. EVIDENCE THAT STRENGTHENS TH…

**Remediation.** Promote both to unconditional checks that fail closed via gic::halt_all (the pattern acquire_kernel_entry already uses at rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs (acquire_kernel_entry)). Both are off the hot path - one runs once per kernel entry, one once per core at boot - so the cost is negligible relative to the failure mode each describes.

## 5. High-severity findings

#### 1. The D8 payoff theorem does not exist, and four of the five live SMP dispatch arms have no `ipcInvariantFull` bundle at all

- **Severity**: high · **Kind**: `unclosed-work` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `docs/planning/IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md (D8 row, "DISPATCH INTEGRATION IN PROGRESS"; "D8 payoff scope: `dispatchWithCap_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` composes, per IPC arm, the cross-core dispatch wrappers … each needs a `_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` built"); also §Sequencing there ("ad…
- **Tree cite**: none - artifact absent (searched SeLe4n/, tests/); live arms at SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchWithCap), 1824, 1935, 1968

**Observed.** `syscallDispatch_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` and `dispatchWithCap_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` return 0 hits across `SeLe4n/` and `tests/`; there is no `dispatchWithCap_preserves_*` or `syscallDispatch_preserves_*` theorem of any kind. The live SMP `.send`/`.call`/`.reply` arms in `SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean` route through `endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore` ( (endpointQueueEnqueue_tcb_queueNext_backward_ne)), `clearWokenReceiverStash` ( (endpointQueueEnqueue_tcb_queueNext_backward_ne)), `endpointCallCrossCoreDispatch` ( (endpointQueueEnqueue_blockStore_establishes_queueNextTargetBlocked)) and `endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatch` ( (endpointQueueEnqueue_blockStore_establishes_queueNextTargetBlocked)) — each of those four has zero `_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` AND zero `_preserves_ipcInvariant` theorems. Only `endpointReceiveDualOnCore` ( (endpointQueueEnqueue_tcb_queueNext_backward_ne)) has a bundle. Separately, no `reachability bundle` exists anywhere (`grep 'def.*[Rr]eachab'` over `SeLe4n/Kernel/` is empty), so the dischargeable pre-state preconditions every de-threaded bundle now carries — `hCallerReady`, `hReceiverReady`, `hWaiterReady`, `hFreshCaller`, `hSendTailFresh`, `hCallerNotReply`, `hCallerNotUnbound`, `hCallerNotRecv`, `hAllBudgetsNone` (see `SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Invariant/Structural/DualQueueMembership.lean (endpointCall_preserves_ipcInvariantFull)`) — are undischarged by anything in the tree.

**Independent verification.** FACTS: fully substantiated, and in one respect understated. (1) `syscallDispatch_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` and `dispatchWithCap_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` return 0 hits across all *.lean in the repo (comments included). Enumerating every declared name via `grep -o "theorem [A-Za-z0-9_']*_preserves_ipcInvariantFull[A-Za-z0-9_']*"` gives 38 theorems, all per-transition or per-core; none at dispatch level. (2) The four live arms are real and uncovered. Uses: SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchWithCap) (`endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore`), (`clearWokenReceiverStash`), (`endpointCallCrossCoreDispatch`), (`endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatch`). Defs: SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSend.lean (endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore), SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (clearWokenReceiverStash), SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointCallDispatch.lean (endpointCallCrossCoreDispatch), SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointReplyDispatch.lean (endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatch). Searching `<name>_preserves|_establishes|_maintains` for each returns zero. (3) WORSE than reported: the inner per-core transitions `endpointSendDualOnCore` and `endpointCallWithCapsOnCore` also have zero `…

**Remediation.** Build the four missing per-wrapper bundles (`endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore`, `clearWokenReceiverStash`, `endpointCallCrossCoreDispatch`, `endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatch` `_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`) over the already-de-threaded per-transition establishers, then build the reachability bundle (a `SystemState` predicate carrying `allTimeoutBudgetsNone` + the running-syscall-thread readiness/freshness facts, with a boot establisher and per-transition preservation) and land `dispatchWithCap_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`. Do NOT close SM10 by narrowing the IPC verification claim in README/SPEC/CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX to match the missing theorem; sequence the payoff as its own PR slice ahead of the v1.0.0 claim writing, or record it as explicit tracked debt with a named closure target.

**PARTIALLY CLOSED at `v0.34.42` (WS-RR RR2).**  The four missing per-wrapper
bundles now exist: `endpointSendDualWithCapsOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/EndpointSendInvariant.lean`, RR2.14),
`clearWokenReceiverStash_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` (`SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean`,
RR2.16) and `endpointCallCrossCoreDispatch_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` /
`endpointReplyCrossCoreDispatch_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/DispatchInvariant.lean`, RR2.6 and RR2.11).  With
`endpointReceiveDualOnCore`'s pre-existing bundle that is five of five **as this
finding enumerated the arms** — the closure audit then re-derived the arm set
from the dispatch code, found the enumeration short (the live receive runs the
WithCaps form, and `.replyRecv` / `.notificationWait` / `.notificationSignal`
commit state too), and addendum 2 below records what was added and what remains
registered.  Building the two dispatch chains meant
first building what they compose over: the donation primitives had **no**
invariant surface at all (`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Invariant/DonationPreservation.lean`,
RR2.5), and the PIP stage both chains end on had none either
(`propagatePipChainCrossCore_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`).  The donation bundle
also *derives* its two preconditions from the `.call` rendezvous rather than
assuming them, which needed a new forward decomposition of
`endpointCallOnCore` — the alternative was to hand the caller two hypotheses it
would have had to discharge by inspection.

Two things this does **not** close, both owned by RR3.

1. **The payoff theorem is still absent.**  `dispatchWithCap_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`
   needs the reachability bundle — a pre-state predicate carrying
   `allTimeoutBudgetsNone` plus the running-thread readiness and freshness
   facts, with a boot establisher and per-transition preservation — and that
   predicate is RR3's, not RR2's.  Per-arm carriage is a prerequisite for it,
   not a substitute; no verification claim in README/SPEC/CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX
   was widened on the strength of it.
2. **One of the bundles is staged, not production — and the count used to be
   two, for a wrong reason.**  The original RR2 landing staged *both* dispatch
   chains' bundles in `DispatchInvariant.lean`, with a rationale naming
   `EndpointCallInvariant` **and** `EndpointReplyInvariant` as the staged
   surfaces it composes.  The closure audit measured the second half false:
   `EndpointReplyInvariant` is production (imported by the live API through
   `EndpointSendInvariant`), so the `.reply` chain's bundle was staged only by
   cohabitation.  The audit split the module: the `.reply` chain's bundle now
   lives in the production `EndpointReplyDispatchInvariant.lean`, the
   priority-inheritance walk's bundle beside its driver in
   `DonationPreservation.lean` §8, and only the `.call` chain's remains staged
   on the genuinely staged `EndpointCallInvariant`.  The same audit found the
   `.receive` arm had been counted covered on the strength of the *bare*
   per-core bundle while the live arm runs the WithCaps form — the same
   measured-the-wrong-function shape as blocker 3 — and built
   `endpointReceiveDualWithCapsOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariantFull{,_perCore}`
   (production), plus bundles for `replyRecvReturnDonation` and the
   `.notificationWait` dispatch wrapper.  Still uncovered at transition level:
   `notificationSignalBoundOnCore` (SM6.D's registered bound-delivery debt) and
   the composition layer (`Checked` wrappers, `replyRecvBody`,
   `Architecture.stage*` frames) that RR3.22 owns.  Finding 9 in this section
   is the same staging shape at the kernel-entry level.

The live op is suspendThreadOnCore (SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/Cancellation.lean (suspendThreadOnCore), inside `namespace Lifecycle.Suspend` opened at (syscallDispatchFromAbi_error_stages_no_frame)), and it genuinely is the live.tcbSuspend target: SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchCapabilityOnly) calls `Lifecycle.Suspend.suspendThreadOnCore st vtid (determineExecutingCore st tid)`; SeLe4n/Kernel/SyscallDispatchEntry.lean (suspendThreadCrossCoreEntry) is the FFI seam; SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (ffiSuspendThread) names it. NO preservation theorem exists for it anywhere in the tree. Whole-repo enumerations: `theorem *_preserves_ipcInvariant` yields 54 names, `*_preserves_objects_invExt` ~90 names, `*_preserves_ipcInvariantFull{,_perCore}` 40 names -- none contains suspendThreadOnCore_* or su…

**Remediation.** Prove `suspendThreadOnCore_preserves_objects_invExt` and `suspendThreadOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariant` by composing ingredients that already exist (cancelIpcBlocking_preserves_ipcInvariant, the propagatePipChainCrossCore frames, both cancel*DonationOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariant, the removeRunnableOnCore/clearPendingState frames, the.Inactive store). The composition is mechanical and every piece is landed. Do not restate the claim more narrowly — state it about the live operation and prove it there.

**CLOSED at `v0.34.42` (WS-RR RR2.17).**  Both theorems the remediation names
now exist and are about the live operation:
`suspendThreadOnCore_preserves_objects_invExt` and
`suspendThreadOnCore_preserves_ipcInvariant`
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/CrossCore/Cancellation.lean`).  The composition was slightly
less mechanical than the finding expected.  `suspendThreadOnCore` is eight
stages, and they preserve `ipcInvariant` for different reasons — some leave
`objects` untouched, the rest write only `.tcb` objects and so transport the
invariant *backwards* through their readings — while each stage needs its
predecessor's `objects.invExt` to state its own preservation, so neither fact
can be threaded alone.  RR2.17 therefore introduced `IpcInvariantStage`: a
reflexive, transitive relation bundling the two, with `of_objects_eq` and
`of_notification_backward` constructors and one instance per stage.  The
composite is then eight `trans` steps, and both public forms project out of the
same chain rather than each re-deriving it.  The module's warning against wiring
the home-keyed composites live is unaffected — the new theorem is about
`suspendThreadOnCore`, which does the running-core handling those composites
lack.  `tests/SmpIpcSuite.lean` §3.13b (RR2.19) covers the arm executably:
local and remote victims, the SGI the remote case raises, and the `illegalState`
rejection of an already-inactive victim.

**Remediation.** Register in SM10.1 an explicit obligation that the `lean_kernel_main` install uses `bootFromPlatformWithIdleThreads`, and land the connecting theorem `∀ c, idleDispatchableOnCore (bootFromPlatformWithIdleThreads config).state c = true` so the live no-stall mechanism is proven reachable from the real boot state rather than assumed. The gate is absent, under any name, and never existed.** - `scripts/test_tier0_hygiene.sh` contains 30 `run_check` invocations and **0** occurrences of the string "tlb" (case-insensitive) — no TLBI check of any kind. (The auditor's "32 run_check/run_negative_check" is slightly off — it is 30 `run_check`, 0 `run_negative_check` in tier-0 — immaterial to the claim.) - Repo-wide grep for `tlbi_vae1|tlbi vae1|ffiTlbiAll|ffiTlbiByAsid|ffiTlbiByVaddr|ffi_tlbi_by_vaddr` over every `*.sh`, `*.py`, `*.yml`, `*.yaml`, `*.toml` outside `docs/` returns **zero hits**. The full `scripts/` listing has no candidate under another name; the closest structural gate, `scripts/check_live_arm_per_core_routing.py`, polices boot-pinned *scheduler* primitives and never mentions TLB. - Tier-3 has 115 `run_negative_check`s but none touch TLBI; its only TLB entries are *positive* anchors (`scripts/test_tier3_invariant_surface.sh (_rg_shim_cleanup)`…

**Remediation.** Implement the gate the plan claims exists rather than softening §4.4. Add a Tier-0 `run_negative_check` (which routes through `scripts/lean_code_view.py --overlay` automatically, so a docstring mentioning the forbidden symbol cannot trip it) that forbids `ffiTlbiAll`, `ffiTlbiByAsid` and `ffiTlbiByVaddr` call sites anywhere under `SeLe4n/` outside a one-line allowlist naming `Concurrency.Runtime.tlbiLocalFullFlush`, with a witness test in the style of `scripts/test_identifier_naming_gate.py`. On the Rust side, make §4.4 true structurally: demote `tlbi_vmalle1`/`tlbi_vae1`/`tlbi_aside1`/`tlbi_vale1` to `pub(crate)` and let `tlbi_local` plus the three FFI exports be the only public surface.

**CLOSED at `v0.34.41` (WS-RR RR1.9).**  Both halves landed.  The gate is
`scripts/check_tlbi_broadcast_discipline.py`, wired into Tier 0 with a
12-case self-test that asserts each defect is caught *and* that a comment
naming a forbidden symbol is not.  It is a Python check rather than a
`run_negative_check` because the Lean half is only one of three invariants:
it also confines the `tlbi` mnemonic itself to `tlb.rs` (nothing else in
the crate or the three `.S` sources may emit one, so no call site can
bypass the wrappers' mandatory `DSB`/`ISB` bracket), and it holds the Rust
call sites to the same allowlist.  The allowlist —
`scripts/tlbi_local_allowlist.txt` — is longer than the sketched one line
because three call shapes are legitimately local, not one: boot-time MMU
init (pre-SMP), the shootdown protocol's receive side (`retire_round_ops_in`
and `self_service_round_in`, where each PE retires its **own** entries and a
hardware broadcast from the receiver would turn one round into N), and the
FFI exports of those two.  It is checked in both directions, so an entry
cannot outlive its call site.  The four local wrappers are now
`pub(crate)`, making §4.4's "private helpers" true structurally as well.

#### 7. D-6's Tier-5 "Rust oracle" is a transliteration of the Lean spec, not the Rust RwLock — the exact STUB hazard §5.6 claims to close

- **Severity**: high · **Kind**: `false-completeness-claim` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `docs/planning/SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md §1 there (D-6 **LANDED**), §5.6 there, §8 there (gate: "zero mismatches"), §9 there ("Eliminated by design... closes audit H-3")
- **Tree cite**: rust/sele4n-hal/src/bin/rw_lock_oracle.rs and (generate_sequences); scripts/test_tier5_cross_language.sh (generate_sequences)

**Observed.** §5.6 (plan there, 1635, 1645) specifies a Rust oracle that "holds a real `RwLock` / `QueuedRwLock` in process memory" and "executes each op against the real lock", and explicitly rejects the alternative because "The harness as written would test a STUB that simulates the lock, not the real impl" (audit M-8). The landed binary does the rejected thing: rust/sele4n-hal/src/bin/rw_lock_oracle.rs is headed "## Why a software model, not the real RwLock?", and its `AbstractState::apply` ((READER_MASK)) is documented as "mirroring the Lean `applyOp` semantics" — it never constructs a `sele4n_hal::rw_lock::RwLock`. I reimplemented both state machines from source and ran the driver's full 1000-sequence corpus: 0 mismatches, because the Rust side is a re-derivation of the Lean side. The harness therefore validates a Lean↔Lean identity and provides zero evidence about `rw_lock.rs`, which is the entire stated purpose of D-6 ("verifies the simulation φ holds", §5.6 plan statement).

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED, and the tree supports a stronger version of the claim than the auditor stated. PLAN CITES ALL VERIFY EXACTLY. - docs/planning/SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md marks D-6 "**LANDED**" unqualified, in the same table that marks D-4 "**PARTIAL**" — so LANDED is a deliberate claim, not shorthand. - specifies the Rust oracle "holds a real `RwLock` / `QueuedRwLock` in process memory... executes each op against the real lock (driving multiple OS threads for concurrent op blocks)". - rejects the alternative precisely because "The harness as written would test a STUB that simulates the lock, not the real impl" (closing audit M-8). - the plan's own Rust skeleton is literally `use sele4n_hal::rw_lock::RwLock; let lock = RwLock::new; lock.acquire_read(...);... lock.peek_state`. - the D-6 acceptance gate demands "zero mismatches". LANDED ARTIFACT DOES THE REJECTED THING. - rust/sele4n-hal/src/bin/rw_lock_oracle.rs: grepping `RwLock` returns lines 2…

**Remediation.** Implement the improvement the plan already specifies: drive the real lock. The blocking-under-contention objection in the oracle's docstring is solvable without abandoning the real impl — either expose non-blocking `try_acquire_*` probes on `rw_lock::RwLock` and have the oracle model *admission* while the real lock supplies *state*, or adopt the plan's own "driving multiple OS threads for concurrent op blocks" design with a join barrier per block. Do not retitle D-6 or weaken §5.6; until the oracle links `sele4n_hal::rw_lock`, D-6 should be re-marked PARTIAL in the §1 table with the real-lock driver as its closure target.

**CLOSED at `v0.34.50` (WS-RR RR6.1–RR6.3), by the first of the two designs
this remediation names.**  `rw_lock::RwLock` gained non-blocking
`try_acquire_read` / `try_acquire_write` — each a single CAS attempt mirroring
exactly one iteration of the blocking loop, so the probe cannot admit where the
loop would not — and the oracle's `Driver` holds **both** real locks (a
`RwLock` and a `QueuedRwLock`) in process memory and drives every generated
operation through them.  The abstract side models *admission* only; the state
the oracle renders is read back from the deployed lock's own word
(`peek_state`), which is what makes the comparison an implementation check
rather than the Lean↔Lean identity the finding measured.  Three relations are
checked after **every** operation, not just at the end: that the two
implementations agree, that the queued lock's `[now_serving, next_ticket)`
interval matches the abstract waiter queue, and that the state word is
`encodeRwLock` of the abstract state.  D-6's "zero mismatches" gate is
therefore met against the real locks; §5.6 stands unweakened.

The RwLock counterpart got the…

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED, and the finding understates the defect. Corrected blocksSm10 to false. WHAT I VERIFIED IN THE TREE 1. The theorem is counter arithmetic, not a simulation. SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/TicketLockRefinement.lean (ticketLockSim) defines `ticketLockSim abstract concrete := concrete.nextTicket.toNat = abstract.nextTicket ∧ concrete.serving.toNat = abstract.serving`. Abstract `pending`/`held` appear in no theorem. `grep -n applyOp` on the file returns only and (ticketLockSim_preserved_by_observeServing), both inside docstrings; `MemoryTrace`/`MemoryEvent` return zero hits. Confirmed. 2. The tautology is real. rust_ticketLock_refines_lean's fourth conjunct (SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/TicketLockRefinement.lean (rust_ticketLock_refines_lean)) is `∀ abs conc, ticketLockSim abs conc → ticketLockSim abs conc`, discharged by `ticketLockSim_preserved_by_observeServing` ( (ticketLockSim_preserved_by_observeServing)) whose proof is literally `:= h_sim`. True independent of φ. 3. STRONGER THAN CLAIMED — the hand-written steps do not merely fail to *use* `applyOp`, they do not *match* it. `TicketLockState.applyOp`…

**Remediation.** Implement the improvement: raise F-01 to the same standard as F-02.
