Do not weaken the plan's F-01 row to match the current theorem. If the full closure genuinely cannot land before v1.0.0, lift it out of the source docstring into a registered debt row (docs/planning/ + CLAUDE.md standing constraints) with an explicit closure target, per the CLAUDE.md rule forbidding deferred items that live only in source comments.

**CLOSED at `v0.34.50` (WS-RR RR6.12–RR6.14).**  F-01 is now built to F-02's
standard: `ConcreteTicketLockOp` and `TicketLockConcrete.applyOp` are the
operational step function, `ticketBlock` is the state-indexed block relation
(one abstract op to one concrete block, with a stutter prefix for the unbounded
`now_serving` spin), and `ticketTrace_preserves_ticketLockSim` composes them
over a chain — stated so it does **not** take its own per-block conclusion as a
hypothesis, which is the defect the sibling finding above records.  The
tautological fourth conjunct is gone: `observeServing` is discharged by a real
"a pure load leaves both states unchanged" statement over the concrete step
(`observeServing_noop`), and `ticketLockSim_not_universal` exhibits a concrete
and an abstract state the relation **does not** relate, so the relation is
falsifiable rather than vacuously true.  The step conjuncts read
`TicketLockState.applyOp` rather than hand-written record updates, so the
mismatch the independent verification found — the hand-written steps did not
merely fail to use `applyOp`, they did not agree with it — cannot recur.

It imports SyscallDispatchEntry with the explicit rationale "so the `@[export]` symbol is emitted into the kernel image" (SeLe4n.lean) and does not import the other three. They are allowl…

**Remediation.** Implement the improvement the tree already documents: promote `SeLe4n.Kernel.SecondaryEntry`, `SeLe4n.Kernel.PerCoreTimerEntry` and `SeLe4n.Kernel.PerCoreRescheduleEntry` into `SeLe4n.lean`'s import closure exactly as `SyscallDispatchEntry` was promoted, and drop their `staged_module_allowlist.txt` entries in the same cut (the allowlist header already documents this promotion pattern for `Locks.RwLock` / `MemoryModel`). #### 11. The SVC and suspend seams call into Lean without the lean_ready gate that kernel_entry.rs claims every seam consults

- **Severity**: high · **Kind**: `false-completeness-claim` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `bootpath`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md §3 SM10.1 (the phase that will call mark_lean_ready and will trust this invariant)
- **Tree cite**: rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs vs rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (dispatch_svc) and rust/sele4n-hal/src/ffi.rs (sele4n_suspend_thread)

**Observed.** rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs states, over the five-entry table there: "Every hardware seam above therefore also consults the per-core readiness gate ([crate::lean_ready]) before its Lean call - a core SM10.1's initialization has not marked ready degrades to its Rust-only half instead of entering a runtime it never initialized." Only three of the five do: rust/sele4n-hal/src/timer.rs (per_core_timer_tick_isr), trap.rs, rust/sele4n-hal/src/smp.rs (rust_secondary_main) each wrap the Lean call in `if lean_ready(core)`. grep for lean_ready in svc_dispatch.rs and ffi.rs returns nothing. rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (dispatch_svc) calls lean_syscall_dispatch_cross_core and rust/sele4n-hal/src/ffi.rs (sele4n_suspend_thread) calls suspend_thread_cross_core unconditionally - and unlike the other three, neither is even hw_target-gated (rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (dispatch_svc) declares the extern under plain #[cfg(not(test))]). lib.rs states the same claim correctly, naming only the three gated seams, so the two module docs disagree with each other.

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED on the facts, downgraded critical -> high on exposure. What the tree says: - rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs claims, over the five-entry table there: "Every hardware seam above therefore also consults the per-core readiness gate ([crate::lean_ready]) before its Lean call". Only three do: rust/sele4n-hal/src/timer.rs (per_core_timer_tick_isr) (`if crate::lean_ready::lean_ready(core_id as usize)` before `lean_per_core_timer_tick` at 434), trap.rs (before `lean_per_core_reschedule` at 510), rust/sele4n-hal/src/smp.rs (rust_secondary_main) (before `lean_secondary_kernel_main` at 735). - `grep -n lean_ready rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs rust/sele4n-hal/src/ffi.rs` returns ZERO hits. rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (dispatch_svc) `dispatch_svc` enters `crate::kernel_entry::with_kernel_entry(core,...)` at 595 and calls `lean_syscall_dispatch_cross_core` (extern declared at 655-657 under plain `#[cfg(not(test))]`) with no readiness check. rust/sele4n-hal/src/ffi.rs (sele4n_suspend_thread) `sele4n_suspend_thread` brackets at 1238 and calls `suspend_thread_cross_core` (extern at rust/sele4n-hal/src/ffi.rs (cache_ic_maintenance), a…

**Remediation.** Implement the gate on both seams so the claim becomes true: in dispatch_svc, check lean_ready(core) before entering the bracket and return a fail-closed label-encoded frame (error_frame_regs) when the core is not ready; in sele4n_suspend_thread, return a nonzero KernelError status. Gate both externs on hw_target for symmetry with the other three. Do not weaken the kernel_entry.rs sentence - the syscall path is the highest-traffic route into the Lean runtime and is exactly what the gate exists to protect.

#### 12. No aarch64 target is compiled anywhere in the tree or CI - 67 cfg-gated blocks, 59 asm! sites and all three.S files have zero compile coverage

- **Severity**: high · **Kind**: `sm10-prerequisite` · **Blocks SM10 start**: no
- **Source plan**: `bootpath`
- **Plan cite**: docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md §2 "Tier 0..5 tests green at HEAD" (a green tier set that never compiles the target architecture)
- **Tree cite**: rust/sele4n-hal/build.rs (main);.github/workflows/lean_action_ci.yml (Rust ABI Tests job, host-only)

**Observed.** rust/sele4n-hal/src carries 67 `#[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]` blocks and 59 asm! sites covering MMU/TTBR/TCR/SCTLR programming (mmu.rs), PSCI SMC (psci.rs), GIC MMIO (gic.rs), TLBI/IC/DC maintenance (tlb.rs, cache.rs) and the register accessors. rust/sele4n-hal/build.rs (main) assembles boot.S, vectors.S and trap.S only when target_arch == "aarch64". Grepping.github/workflows/*.yml for aarch64 or `rustup target add` returns nothing; the only Rust job runs scripts/test_rust.sh on the host. `rustup target list --installed` in rust/ shows only x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu. So the half of the HAL that SM10.1 depends on most has never been type-checked, borrow-checked, or assembled by any gate.

**Independent verification.** CONFIRMED, and the tree is worse than the auditor claimed. I tried hard to refute this and instead found a hard build defect. ## The structural claims all check out, exactly - `rust/sele4n-hal/src`: `#[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]` attributes = **67** (12 barriers.rs, 12 tlb.rs, 9 cache.rs, 9 cpu.rs, 7 psci.rs…), `asm!` sites = **59**, three `.S` files (boot.S, vectors.S, trap.S). 27 `cfg(not(target_arch = "aarch64"))` host stubs are the arms that actually compile on x86_64 — cfg-false arms are parsed but never type/borrow-checked. - `rust/sele4n-hal/build.rs (main)` gates all three `.S` on `CARGO_CFG_TARGET_ARCH == "aarch64"` and returns early otherwise; `cc::Build` at 157-161. - `.github/workflows/*.yml`: the only `aarch64` token anywhere is `qemu-system-aarch64 --version` at `nightly_determinism.yml`. No `rustup target add`, no `--target`. `lean_action_ci.yml` (`test-rust` job) runs only `./scripts/test_rust.sh` on ubuntu-latest/x86_64. - No tier script (`test_fast/s…

**Remediation.** Add an aarch64-unknown-none `cargo build` (plus `cargo clippy`) job to lean_action_ci.yml as SM10's first cut. It is cheap, needs no image target and no QEMU, and it is the prerequisite that makes every later SM10.1 step debuggable rather than a wall of first-compile errors.

**CLOSED at `v0.34.41` (WS-RR RR1).** The remediation landed as written and
then some: `scripts/test_aarch64_cross_build.sh` runs a debug *and* release
`cargo build --target aarch64-unknown-none -p sele4n-hal --features
hw_target`, verifies the three `.S` sources really assembled, and runs
`cargo clippy -D warnings` on the same target; the `aarch64 Cross Build` job
in `lean_action_ci.yml` invokes it on every PR; and
`scripts/check_aarch64_cross_target.py` (Tier 0) keeps the configuration
from being dropped or weakened.  §5.1 records what the first compile found.

### 5.1 The measured aarch64 surface (WS-RR RR1.10, `v0.34.41`)

Finding 12 above sized the uncovered surface from a raw-text grep.  RR1
compiled it, so the surface is now measured rather than estimated.  Two of
finding 12's three figures are confirmed exactly — 67 cfg-gated blocks and
three `.S` sources — and the third, the `asm!` count, moves.  Everything
below is reproducible; the commands are given so a later reader can
re-measure rather than trust a number.

**The surface, at `v0.34.40` (the state finding 12 describes) and after RR1:**

| Measure | `v0.34.40` | post-RR1 | Note |
|---------|-----------:|---------:|------|
| `.rs` sources under `rust/sele4n-hal/src` | 30 | 30 | includes the one host tool in `src/bin/` |
| `.S` sources | 3 | 3 | `boot.S` (310 lines), `vectors.S` (123), `trap.S` (209) |
| `#[cfg(target_arch = "aarch64")]` | **67** | 68 | finding 12's figure, confirmed exactly |
| `#[cfg(not(target_arch = "aarch64"))]` | 26 | 28 | the host stubs — the arms the host lane compiles |
| compound `cfg` naming `aarch64` | 3 | 3 | `all(...)` / `any(...)` forms |
| `#[cfg(feature = "hw_target")]` | 7 | 7 | `boot.rs` 1, `smp.rs` 2, `timer.rs` 2, `trap.rs` 2 |
| `asm!` invocations | **57** | 57 | finding 12 says 59 — see below |
| `read_sysreg!` / `write_sysreg!` call sites | 39 | 40 | each expands to one further `asm!` |
| `asm!` instances after macro expansion | 96 | 97 | what the assembler actually sees |

**The `asm!` figure was 59 because the count read prose.**  `rust/sele4n-hal/src`
holds 59 occurrences of the token `asm!` and **57 invocations**: the other
two are docstring mentions, in `cpu.rs` ("via inline `asm!` blocks") and
`psci.rs` ("is just `asm!` + this decoder").
[`SMP_RELEASE_READINESS_PLAN.md`](SMP_RELEASE_READINESS_PLAN.md)'s RR1
section then compounded it to 60, a third value matching neither.  The
measured figure is
**57**, and the discrepancy is the project's own "gates read code, prose
reads prose" rule appearing in the audit that was written to enforce it.

Reproduce (from the repository root):

```bash
# Strip `//` comments, then count.  Reading raw text is what produced 59.
python3 - <<'EOF'
import os, re
def code(t): return "\n".join((l if (i:=l.find("//"))<0 else l[:i]) for l in t.splitlines())
root, cfg, asm = "rust/sele4n-hal/src", 0, 0
for d, _, fs in os.walk(root):
    for f in sorted(fs):
        if not f.endswith(".rs"): continue
        c = code(open(os.path.join(d, f), encoding="utf-8").read())
        cfg += len(re.findall(r'#\[cfg\(target_arch\s*=\s*"aarch64"\)\]', c))
        asm += len(re.findall(r'\basm!\s*\(', c))
print("cfg-gated blocks:", cfg, " asm! invocations:", asm)
EOF
```

**What the first compile found.** Six defects, in three classes.  None was
reachable by any gate that existed before RR1, and three of the six are
invisible to `cargo check` because it stops before code generation:

| # | Site | Class | Defect |
|---|------|-------|--------|
| 1 | `boot.S` (`_start`, step 4) | assembly | `and sp, sp, #~0xF` — `AND (immediate)` accepts SP as destination but **not** as source (ARM ARM C6.2.13), so the primary's stack-alignment step does not assemble |
| 2 | `boot.S` (`secondary_entry`, step 2) | assembly | the same instruction on the secondary bring-up path |
| 3–6 | `tlb.rs` (`tlbi_vmalle1os`, `tlbi_vae1os`, `tlbi_aside1os`, `tlbi_vale1os`) | codegen | `TLBI VMALLE1OS / VAE1OS / ASIDE1OS / VALE1OS` are **FEAT_TLBIOS** (ARMv8.4-A) and do not encode for the baseline target — nor execute on **Cortex-A76**, the ARMv8.2-A core in the RPi5's BCM2712 |
| — | `profiling.rs` (the `core::sync::atomic` import) | lint | `unused_imports`: `AtomicU64` and `Ordering` are used only by the `not(aarch64)` stub counter |
| — | `boot.rs` (`install_exception_vectors`) | lint | `unused_unsafe`: `&raw const` on an extern static forms an address without accessing it |
| — | `gic.rs` (`read_self_check_target`) | lint | `clippy::needless_return` in a block the host lane removes before clippy sees it |

Defects 3–6 were the substantive find.  The four `*OS` wrappers now probe
`ID_AA64ISAR0_EL1.TLB` and take `cpu::fatal_halt()` on a PE that does not
implement the feature — deliberately *not* falling back to the IS variant,
which would service only the inner-shareable domain while the caller asked
for the outer one.  The mnemonics assemble under a scoped
`.arch_extension tlb-rmi` / `notlb-rmi` bracket rather than by raising the
whole crate's target features to v8.4, which would let the compiler emit
v8.4 instructions anywhere on a target that cannot run them.  A `build.rs`
scanner pins both properties, since a dropped restore fails *open*.

**Two lanes now cover the crate between them.**  The host lane compiles the
26 `not(aarch64)` stubs; the cross lane compiles the 67 aarch64 blocks, all
57 `asm!` sites (97 after expansion) and all three `.S` files.  Before RR1
the second set had no lane at all — and, because `scripts/test_rust.sh`
lints only the host target, the project's zero-clippy-warning claim excluded
the entire hardware surface.

**A seventh finding, outside the count.**  `src/bin/rw_lock_oracle.rs` — the
Tier-5 correspondence oracle, a `std` host tool — was being compiled for the
bare-metal target and failed with 35 errors, which is what made the crate
look uncross-compilable at all.  It now carries a `required-features =
["host_tools"]` gate.

## 7. Low-severity findings

99 confirmed. The table is *not* uniformly documentation drift, and §7.1 below
triages every row by what closes it: 20 were closed outright by the WS-RR RR0
cut, 9 more by registering them in the project debt register, 18 belong to a
phase already reworking the same artefact, **15 need code, a proof, a test or a
wiring change and became RR7 rows (RR7.33–RR7.37)**, and 37 are genuinely
SM10.2's documentation work-list.

### 7.1 Triage by remedy (WS-RR RR0.11, `v0.34.26`)

Low severity means the *consequence* is small.  It does not mean the *remedy*
is a sentence, and handing all 99 rows to a documentation sweep would have let
real work reach release closure as prose — four per-core statistics accessors
declared, wrapped and proven with no consumer are not a stale sentence, and a
live docstring citing a theorem that does not exist is not a typo.

Every row is therefore routed by what closes it, not by its severity.  The
five destinations below partition the 99: **20 + 9 + 18 + 15 + 37 = 99**.

| Destination | Rows | Count |
|-------------|------|-------|
| **1. Closed by the RR0 cut** — the defect no longer exists at `v0.34.26` | 13, 18, 48, 49, 57, 75, 76, 79, 80, 81, 82, 83, 86, 88, 90, 91, 92, 94, 95, 97 | 20 |
| **2. Closed by registration** — the defect *was* the absence of a durable entry; each now has one in the *Registered debt index* of [`../REGISTERED_DEBT.md`](../REGISTERED_DEBT.md), with an owner and a closure target | 5, 10, 11, 24, 27, 36, 37, 43, 73 | 9 |
| **3. Already owned by a numbered row** — the owning phase closes it in passing, because it is retiring or reworking the same artefact.  RR3: 1, 2, 3.  RR4: 9, 96.  RR6: 34, 54, 55, 56, 58, 64, 65, 66.  RR7.7–RR7.13: 14, 16, 17.  RR7.30: 46.  SM10.4: 19 | 1, 2, 3, 9, 14, 16, 17, 19, 34, 46, 54, 55, 56, 58, 64, 65, 66, 96 | 18 |
| **4. New RR7 rows** — needs code, a proof, a test or a wiring change, and had no owner.  RR7.33: 15, 98.  RR7.34: 6, 26, 71, 77, 93.  RR7.35: 22, 28, 78, 87.  RR7.36: 42.  RR7.37: 59, 74, 84 | 6, 15, 22, 26, 28, 42, 59, 71, 74, 77, 78, 84, 87, 93, 98 | 15 |
| **5. SM10.2's documentation work-list** — genuinely prose: a stale count, a renamed symbol in a landing note, a plan section describing a design the tree replaced | 4, 7, 8, 12, 20, 21, 23, 25, 29, 30, 31, 32, 33, 35, 38, 39, 40, 41, 44, 45, 47, 50, 51, 52, 53, 60, 61, 62, 63, 67, 68, 69, 70, 72, 85, 89, 99 | 37 |

**Destination 5 is cross-referenced from
[`SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md`](SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md) §3 SM10.2**, so
the sweep has a work-list rather than an instruction to re-audit.  Four of its
rows (41, 52, 62, 70) are **source comments** rather than documents — a
`cmdline.rs` docstring that contradicts the code it documents, a `Staged.lean`
note describing the live syscall entry as staged via an export WS-RA deleted —
and they are prose only in the sense that the fix is a comment; SM10.2 must
read the code beside each before editing it.

**Why some rows are in destination 3 rather than 5.**  A row that corrects a
plan an earlier phase *retires* is that phase's, not the sweep's: RR3.26 moves
the de-threading plan to `dev_history`, so rows 1–3 must be right before it
moves rather than corrected after.  The same holds for RR6's plan corrections
(RR6.25) and RR4's `trap.rs` rewrite.

**Rows the triage deliberately did not route to a sweep.**  Row 98 (four
per-core statistics accessors with zero consumers) and row 15 (`ipcUnwrapCaps`'s
dead `senderCspaceRoot`, whose own registered closure target passed without it)
are implement-the-improvement cases: a computed-and-proven structure the
surrounding code does not consume gets wired in, never deleted and never
documented as absent.  Row 22 ("every `dev_history` cross-reference removed from
production sources" — nine remain, and no gate enforces the claim) is a claim
with no mechanism, which is the same shape as the tier-4 gates that scored a
skip as a pass.

### `docs/planning/WS_RC_R4_TYPE_LEVEL_PROMOTION_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: **Status**: **COMPLETE** — both R4.A (`UniqueSlotMap`-backed `CNode.slots`) and R4.C (`NoDupList ThreadId`-backed `Notification.waitingThreads`) landed; foundation modules + field-type switches + all consumer migrations + tests all green.

This plan promotes two state-level invariants (`cspaceSlotUnique`, `uniqueWaiters`) into type-level structural invariants carried by two new wrapper structures. The COMPLETE claim holds up under aggressive verification: both foundation modules exist with their full stated API surfaces, both field-type switches landed (`CNode.slots : SeLe4n.UniqueSlotMap Capability`, `Notification.waitingThreads : SeLe4n.NoDupList SeLe4n.ThreadId`), the operational rewire (`consWithGuard?`, `tail?`, `filter`) is live in Endpoint/Cleanup/FrozenOps, both state-level predicates are genuinely *deleted* (not aliased to `True`) with bundle arities updated, and every discharge-index reachability `#check` elaborates. I independently ran `lake build` (436 jobs green), `./scripts/test_smoke.sh` (all-green), `./scripts/test_tier0_hygiene.sh` (all-green), `tests/ModelIntegritySuite.lean` (all passed), and confirmed `lake exe sele4n` is byte-identical to the golden trace fixture. Nothing here blocks starting SM10 — `SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` contains no reply-object item at all — but the false residual-debt claim should be corrected before v1.0.0 so a future reply-cap change does not re-apply the reverted "rights-less" design.

### `docs/planning/SMP_INFORMATION_FLOW_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: "SM8.A COMPLETE at v0.33.3... SM8.B LANDED at v0.33.5; SM8.C LANDED at v0.33.7... SM8.D LANDED at v0.33.9... **SM8.E LANDED at v0.33.23 — the phase is CLOSED**" (plan header, lines 8-14); all 13 acceptance-gate boxes checked (§8)
- **Verified**: **complete-with-registered-debt**

SM8 covers per-core observable state (SM8.A), per-core and cross-core non-interference (SM8.B), the per-core declassification audit and its producer (SM8.C), information flow under fine locks with the CC-5 lock-contention channel bounded (SM8.D), and the anchors/fixture/boundary-promotion closure (SM8.E). The CLOSED claim holds up unusually well against the tree: every one of the 18 theorem names in §6.1 resolves as a real declaration and every one is anchored in tests/SmpSurfaceAnchors.lean; all 948 SM8-related Tier-3 anchors pass when evaluated through the project's own comment-free code-view overlay; the six SM8 modules plus their SM9 successors sweep axiom-clean over 4774 environment constants; both golden fixtures hash-match; and the per-core routing allowlist is genuinely empty. Of the 12 "open" tokens in the body, nine are domain vocabulary or historical fail-open defects already closed, and three are live: the class-C capacity-figure single-sourcing follow-on, the rounds-39/40 unbind reschedule re-gating decision, and registered debt (b) — the live `.send` arm's missing IPC invariant preservation. That last one is the substantive finding: SM8 honestly registers it, but the closure target it names ("SM6.D's open bundle-carriage list") does not carry it, and SM6.D instead affirmatively claims the send is covered by citing a theorem about the boot-pinned single-core function that stopped being the live arm at v0.33.5. Two further SM2.C debts raised by SM8.D's review cuts point at a phase that is CLOSED and appear in no live plan. None of these stops SM10 from starting; the send-preservation gap is the one SM10 must absorb before a v1.0.0 "every kernel transition machine-checked" claim.

### `docs/planning/SMP_PER_CORE_SCHEDULER_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: "Status: LANDED (v0.31.38 → v0.31.64) — sub-phases A–K per the in-body landing notes"; §8 acceptance gate shows 6 of 10 boxes checked, 4 unchecked; every sub-phase landing note ends "Items deferred past v1.0.0 with correctness impact: NONE."
- **Verified**: **complete-with-registered-debt**

SM5 is the strongest plan I have audited on this tree in terms of artifact reality: every named Lean module, operational def, Rust seam, test suite, golden fixture and QEMU stub exists, the modules build clean, all four SM5 suites I ran pass, the golden fixture's sha256 matches, and `#print axioms` confirms the headline theorems are foundational-axiom-clean with `wcrt_bound_rpi5_smp` genuinely axiom-free. The plan's own documented errata (the §3.8 naming collision, the §3.4 superseded pseudocode after the audit-pass-2 CRITICAL FIX, the `Option Priority` realisation in §3.6) are honest and check out. Where the plan is wrong it is wrong in both directions at once: three of the four unchecked acceptance boxes are stale *pessimistic* — the live cross-core SGI firing, the live per-core timer-tick driver and the live cross-core PIP wiring all landed after the plan was written (SM5.I v0.32.142 and SM6) — while two checked boxes are stale *optimistic*. The two optimistic ones are what matters for SM10: the liveness capstone `no_starvation_under_smp` is cited unqualified in §8, in the SM5.J note and in `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md` even though its `hBandProgress` premise is an unconstructed deployment hypothesis that carries the progress content (CLAUDE.md explicitly requires citations to state it), and its no-stall conjunct rests on `idleThreadEnqueuedOnCore`, a predicate that no boot path establishes, no transition preserves, and that is in fact unsatisfiable on any state where idle is current. Separately, §3.9's "≈1 ms, fits the tick budget" WCRT conclusion is stated without the SM5.I global-entry-ticket-lock caveat that `PerCoreWcrt.lean`'s own module docstring states plainly. None of this makes SM5's work unreal — the live kernel does not stall, because `idleFallbackOnCore` uses a weaker and reachable predicate — but SM10 must not carry these claims into a v1.0.0 release note unchanged, and SM10.1 must consciously pick `bootFromPlatformWithIdleThreads` for the boot seam or the idle dispatch is dead on hardware.

### `docs/planning/SMP_PER_OBJECT_LOCKS_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: "CLOSED (v0.31.9) — per-object locks, lock sets, 2PL, deadlock-freedom, serializability; SM3.C.9 @[export] wrapping deferred (see SMP_FINE_LOCK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md)" (line 4). §8 acceptance gate: 12 of 13 boxes checked, only SM3.C.9 left open.
- **Verified**: **complete-with-registered-debt**

SM3 is the strongest plan I have audited in this portfolio: every headline artefact it claims is genuinely in the tree and machine-checked. `deadlockFreedom_under_2pl_and_ordering`, `waitGraph_acyclic_under_2pl`, `boundedWait_under_2pl`, `serializability_under_2pl`, `strictly_2pl_preserved`, `singleCore_proof_preservation`, `lockOrder_strict`, the `lockAcquireSequence_*` trio and `withLockSet_invariant_preserved` all exist with substantive (non-vacuous, hypothesis-grounded) statements; every named module and all five test suites exist, build (.olean present) and are executed in tier 2; zero sorry/axiom in the whole `Locks/` tree. The plan's honesty about SM3.C.9 is real and the deferral is durably registered in `SMP_FINE_LOCK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`, CLAUDE.md and the Lean `UncoveredLockDomain` registry. Three things nonetheless fall short of the CLOSED claim: `lockSetTransitions_within_bound` covers only 30 of the 34 `lockSet_*` declarations that now exist, leaving four transitions with no bounded-wait/WCRT premise; the SM3.A.11 base case is vacuous on the empty default state and the stronger post-boot form both the plan and the source docstring promise was never written and is registered nowhere durable; and §5.2's "the lockSets now declare exactly the set of objects the underlying transition may write" is contradicted at HEAD by three still-open SM3.B-owned uncovered lock domains. Separately, SM3's deferral propagates into SM10: the release-closure plan's v1.0.0 CHANGELOG skeleton already asserts fine locks as a shipped capability and SMP-C3 as closed, while 32 of 33 syscalls still resolve to `none` in `lockSetForSyscall` and zero `@[export]` bodies in `Platform/FFI.lean` are bracketed. SM10 can start, but must not ship that sentence unchanged.

**Tree evidence checked:**

- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/Deadlock.lean (waitGraph_acyclic_under_2pl) `waitGraph_acyclic_under_2pl` (N-core dual) and (conflictWaitGraph_acyclic_under_2pl) `conflictWaitGraph_acyclic_under_2pl` (mode-aware refinement) both present, as the plan's SM3.D.5 row and its adaptations note claim.
- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/Deadlock.lean (boundedWait_under_2pl) `boundedWait_under_2pl` proves `noDeadlock e ∧ WCRT e c op tCs ≤ maxLockSetSize * ((numCores-1) * tCs)`; `maxLockSetSize := 8` at SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/Deadlock.lean (maxLockSetSize); the WCRT conjunct genuinely consumes `op.sizeWithinBound` and `contendersAhead_le`, so it is contention-sensitive rather than a constant.

### `docs/planning/SMP_TLB_SHOOTDOWN_PLAN.md`

SM7 delivers the explicit-ack TLB shootdown protocol, the per-core TLB model, and the instruction/data cache maintenance broadcast, and its LANDED claims hold up unusually well against the tree. Every named module, headline theorem, Rust function, script and fixture I sampled exists in code (not just in comments); both suites run green at HEAD (smp_tlb_shootdown_suite: 353 assertions / 39 groups, golden fixture sha256 verifies; smp_cache_maintenance_suite: 131 / 15); zero sorry, axiom or native_decide on the SM7 surface; and SM7.D is genuinely wired Lean → FFI → Rust → ARM64 asm, so "CLOSED (model level)" understates rather than overstates what landed. The 33 pending / 18 deferred tokens are overwhelmingly narrative about work that has since closed, not open items. The real problems are routing failures in the debt register rather than missing engineering: SM7.D registers four obligations "against SM10.1" that the SM10 release-closure plan does not carry at all, and two further SM7 debts name SM8 as their closure target — a phase that closed at v0.33.23 without absorbing them. One of those four, the PA-used-as-VA cache operand, is contradicted by an unqualified identity-map claim in `Platform/FFI.lean` and goes live the moment a 4/8 GB Pi 5 boots, so it is a genuine SM10 prerequisite the receiving phase currently cannot see.

### `docs/planning/SYSCALL_RETURN_ABI_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: "COMPLETE — core LANDED at v0.33.37; RA.B.5b + RA.B.8 LANDED at v0.33.38." Both return orderings staged end to end; per-arm shape-coherence family proven; what remains "owed to SM10.1, not to this workstream" (frame delivery at context restore + cancellation/timeout error-frame staging, §9). All ten §8 acceptance-gate boxes checked.
- **Verified**: **complete-with-registered-debt**

WS-RA replaced the pre-existing bit-63 status-word protocol with seL4's ARM64 return frame: x0 = full-width primary result, x1 = MessageInfo whose offset label carries the KernelError (label 0 = success, d+1 = discriminant d), x2-x5 = message registers. This is genuinely landed and unusually well-evidenced. Every headline artefact I checked exists and does what the plan says: SyscallReturn.lean's total syscallReturnShape with its list-gate refutation, the offset-label family with its non-aliasing and 20-bit-fit theorems, SYSCALL_ABI_VERSION = 2 pinned three independent ways (Lean decide theorem, HAL compile-time const assert, abi-crate conformance) with a Tier-3 anchor, the retired encodeOk/encodeError/syscall_dispatch_inner surface held down by Tier-3 negative anchors, trap.rs restoring all six registers, the fail-closed blocked_resume_sentinel_regs poison, and the per-core return-frame mailbox keyed consistently on the TPIDR logical id. I ran the evidence rather than reading about it: the syscall_return_abi_suite exits 0 with 49 assertions and a byte-matching golden fixture, cargo test -p sele4n-abi --test conformance is 108/108, the whole Lean code view is sorry/axiom-free, and check_version_sync passes at 0.34.1. Two of the §10 catalogue's headline theorems (blockingArm_returns_no_frame, notificationWait_delivers_badge_signal_first) were nevertheless never authored under any name — both properties are witnessed at runtime but not pinned as theorems — and the Rust ReturnShape mirror reintroduces the exact list/wildcard shape §3.4 exists to forbid, with nothing cross-checking it against Lean. The more consequential issue is downstream: WS-RA correctly hands SM10.1 two named obligations, but SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md registers them only in its Dependencies prose and schedules neither — SM10.1's sub-task table is QEMU validation only. Since.call always blocks, a v1.0.0 image built without that seam returns UnknownKernelError from every call.

The boot path is honest about itself at the code level and dishonest at the plan level. Everything below the Lean seam is real and well built: boot.S/_start, link.ld, four-phase Rust init, PSCI secondary bring-up, GIC/timer/SGI registration, the SM5.I kernel-entry ticket lock (all five bracketed entries verified present), the fail-closed blocked-resume sentinel, and 67/67 Lean @[extern] symbols resolving to Rust definitions. But nothing above that seam exists: lean_kernel_main is declared in boot.rs and defined nowhere; there is no [[bin]], no aarch64 build in the tree or CI, no rule producing the libsele4n.a that boot.rs says it links, no Lean-runtime initialization, allocator or heap for bare metal, no RPi5 PlatformConfig, no root task, no TTBR0 binding and no context switch (ffi_switch_to_thread stores a u64 into an atomic). All 16 QEMU scripts are unconditional SKIPs gated on a kernel image that cannot be built, so the README's "QEMU boot COMPLETE" has never been observed once. The gap that matters most is not any single missing artifact but that SM10's plan says release closure "has no room for kernel work" while SM10.1 in fact owes bare-metal Lean runtime hosting, the context-restore seam (three named prerequisites, none started), the image target and eight scripts — a body of kernel engineering the 4-6 week estimate does not cover. ### `docs/planning/IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: No header status line. The `## Status` table (there) marks D0 / D2 / D2′ / D5 / D7 as "✅ LANDED" or "✅ FULLY DE-THREADED", D3 as "✅ DE-THREADING COMPLETE: 15/15 bundles", D4 as "Slice 2c COMPLETE / zero threading sites remain", and D1 / D6 / D8 as "🔄 IN PROGRESS". The plan therefore self-describes as an ACTIVE, materially incomplete workstream.
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

This plan de-threads the ~20 post-state structural conjuncts of `ipcInvariantFull` from every IPC `*_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` bundle so the transitions *establish* the invariant rather than assume it. Its verified-landed portion is unusually solid: `ipcInvariantFull` really carries 20 conjuncts (Defs.lean) with a matching 20-field `IpcInvariantFull` structure, the three conjuncts this plan added (`donationOwnerUnique`, `endpointQueueTailBlockedConsistent`, `queueNextTargetBlocked`) exist, every one of ~30 sampled named lemmas exists in code (not just comments), ten separate "zero threading sites" claims (hQNBC'/hEQTB'/hQHBC'/hQNTB'/hPRR'/hDCA'/hBRT'/hDBT'/hPSI'/hBlockedTimeout') each verify to exactly 0 occurrences, and both Findings F-1 and F-3 were remediated with real code/model fixes in the implement-the-improvement direction rather than doc weakening. But the plan is NOT complete and does not claim to be: D1, D6 and D8 are open. Two of the twenty conjuncts are still assumed as post-state hypotheses on nearly every bundle — `blockedThreadsPendingMessageConsistent` on 33 of 35 bundles and `replyCallerLinkageReciprocal` (half of conjunct #16) on 31 of 35 — plus `dualQueueSystemInvariant`/`badgeWellFormed` on 8 sites and `donationOwnerValid` on 6. The headline payoff (`syscallDispatch_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` / `dispatchWithCap_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`) does not exist, the "reachability bundle" that is supposed to discharge every remaining pre-state precondition does not exist, and the donation primitives on the live `.call` path carry no invariant-preservation theorem at all. Most consequentially for the business question: this open workstream is referenced from no durable registry — not CLAUDE.md, not `docs/REGISTERED_DEBT.md`, not the SMP master plan, not `SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md`, not `CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md` — only from historical CHANGELOG `Refs:` lines, so SM10 would close WS-SM at v1.0.0 with it silently dropped.

**Closure note (WS-RR RR3.1–RR3.14, `v0.34.43`).**  The de-threading half is
done and the register's own figures were an undercount.  RR3.1's gate — which
derives the conjunct set from `def ipcInvariantFull`, the bundle family from the
declaration names, and each bundle's pre-state from its own hypothesis, over the
comment-free code view — measured **103 post-state bindings over six
conjuncts** at the baseline, where this entry names two plus "8 sites" and "6
sites".  All six are now zero across all sixty-five statements in the
`*_preserves_ipcInvariantFull*` / `*_establishes_ipcInvariantFull*` family.

Two of the six were **vacuity** defects rather than proof gaps, and neither was
visible to a binder-name census:

* the `*WithCaps` `dualQueueSystemInvariant` theorems carried a receiver-root
  CNode side condition which, quantified over the wrapper, reads "every ObjId is
  a CNode" — false in any state holding the endpoint the step requires, so those
  theorems and the `ipcInvariantFull` bundles above them asserted nothing on
  their own success path; and
* `donationOwnerValid` is **false** of a bare reply's post-state, because
  `endpointReply` wakes the answered caller `.ready` while the recorded server
  still holds `.donated _ caller` and the donation returns at the next stage.  So
  the nine reply bundles threading it were vacuous on the ordinary seL4-MCS path.
  Closed by `donationOwnerValidExcept` plus the donation return's upgrade;
  the live cross-core `.reply` dispatch now covers the donating path.

A third gap surfaced with them: `badgeWellFormed` constrains badges *at rest* in
a CNode while the IPC transfer installs an in-flight capability verbatim, so an
out-of-range in-flight badge became an out-of-range badge at rest.  Closed by the
new pre-state `pendingMessageCapBadgesWellFormed`.

The "reachability bundle" this entry names as missing now exists
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Invariant/Reachability.lean`), with the boot state proved to
satisfy it, and the running-caller and queue-tail preconditions *derived* from
`ipcInvariantFull` itself rather than assumed.

**Re-scoped, then closed (WS-RR RR3.15–RR3.26, `v0.34.43`)**: the headline
payoff.  Its original two rows assumed per-arm bundles that did not exist —
`dispatchWithCap` routes twenty-five syscalls across six subsystems and six of
about thirty arms carried an `ipcInvariantFull` bundle at all — so
RR3.15–RR3.23 built them
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Invariant/DispatchArmPreservation.lean`, production) and
RR3.24/RR3.25 composed them: `dispatchCapabilityOnly_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean`, production) plus
`dispatchWithCap_preserves_ipcInvariantFull` and
`dispatchSyscall_preserves_ipcInvariantFull`
(`SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Invariant/DispatchPayoff.lean`, staged with the `.call`
surface they compose), all under pre-state quiescence packs.  The pending
register [`ipc_dethreading_pending.txt`](ipc_dethreading_pending.txt) is empty
and the gate, which checks it in both directions, now measures 146 statements
at zero post-state bindings and prints its end-to-end PASS line.  The family
grew from sixty-five to 146 with the per-arm and checked tiers.  (`syscallDispatch` named
nothing in the tree; the dispatcher is `dispatchSyscall`, and the theorem is
named for it.)  The plan itself is retired to `docs/dev_history/planning/`
(RR3.26); WS-DT is **CLOSED** with five registered follow-up debt rows in
`docs/REGISTERED_DEBT.md`.

The three conjuncts this plan added (18th/19th/20th) are real defs at Defs.lean, 1429, 1457.

### `docs/planning/SMP_FINE_LOCK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: No `**Status**:` header line at all — unique among the SMP planning docs (every other landed SMP_*.md carries one). The body claims: §1 item 1 "**CLOSED at v0.33.88 across five cuts — see §3.1 for the closure record**; items 2 and 3 below remain open"; §3 header "**CLOSED at v0.33.59**" + "Residual, closed at v0.33.60"; §9.1 "**CLOSED at v0.33.80**"; §9.2 "**Closure target**: still open". All six…
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

This plan covers three coupled closures: the High-severity IPC revocation-precision defect (Track A), the registered `capTransferReceiverCnode` footprint gap (Track B), and the deferred SM3.C.9 `withLockSet` migration plus commit partitioning (Tracks C/D), across 12 PRs. Only Track A (PRs 1-2) has landed — and it landed well: the fix is real, deeper than planned, and durably recorded in REGISTERED_DEBT, CHANGELOG and Lean theorems, with zero sorry/axiom. PRs 3-12 (Tracks B, C, D) are entirely unstarted at HEAD: `lockSet_endpointSend` has no caps optional, `lockSetForSyscall` still returns `none` for all 33 non-suspend arms, `syscallDispatchCrossCoreEntry` has no `withLockSet` bracket, there is no export-body CI gate, no `OBJECT_STRIPE_POOL`, no `transition_footprint_local`, and `modifyGetKernelState` is still a bare `kernelStateRef.modifyGet`. So 5 of the 6 acceptance boxes are genuinely unmet and the plan is honest in leaving them unchecked — this is real open work, not a false claim. It does not block SM10 from *starting* (SM5.I's global entry ticket lock provides live safety today, and `fineLockDisciplineComplete_is_false` is pinned in Lean so nothing may rely on fine locks), but it does block one line of the v1.0.0 closure entry SM10 plans to publish, and the plan's own §3.1 closure record is two cuts stale in a way that overstates a superseded security guarantee.

**Tree evidence checked:**

- Track A §3 fix is real and live: `ipcUnwrapCapsLoop` takes `caps : Array TransferCap` and records the derivation edge from `tc.srcNode`, with the synthetic slot-0 literal gone — SeLe4n/Kernel/IPC/Operations/CapTransfer.lean (ipcUnwrapCapsLoop).
- `ipcTransferSingleCap_installed_implies_live_source` (SeLe4n/Kernel/Capability/Operations.lean (ipcTransferSingleCap_installed_implies_live_source)) and its stronger successor `ipcTransferSingleCap_installed_implies_revocable_source` ( (shift9_extracts_label)) both exist as real theorems, not comment mentions; `CapTransferResult.sourceRevoked` is a real constructor (SeLe4n/Model/Object/Types.lean).
- The revocation half of the guarantee exists and is proven: `revokePendingTransfersFrom` (SeLe4n/Kernel/Capability/Operations.lean (revokePendingTransfersFrom)), `revokePendingTransfersFrom_frame`, `revokePendingTransfersFrom_preserves_capabilityInvariantBundle` (SeLe4n/Kernel/Capability/Invariant/Preservation/Revoke.lean (revokePendingTransfersFrom_preserves_capabilityInvariantBundle)), consumed at Operations.lean and 1942.
- The shared destroyer-side guard `slotIsDerivationParent` is real and shared across the delete guard and CNode-retype arm — SeLe4n/Kernel/Capability/Operations.lean + SeLe4n/Kernel/Lifecycle/Operations/CleanupPreservation.lean.

- §9.1's closure is real: the live `.receive` and `.replyRecv` arms both run `endpointReceiveDualWithCapsOnCore` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (replyRecvBody), 1880, 2331), `replyRecvBodyWriteSet` exists (SeLe4n/Kernel/InformationFlow/NonInterferenceCrossCore.lean (replyRecvBodyWriteSet)), and the `chain12d` arrival-ordering regression runs at tests/OperationChainSuite.lean (replyRecvCapTransferArrivalOrder).

- **Claimed**: "IN EXECUTION (overview) — unified workstream closing at v1.0.0. WS-RC and WS-SM are merged. SM0–SM9 and WS-RA have landed (§9 per-phase table); SM10 (release closure) is the remaining phase."

This is the WS-SM master overview/index: 11 phase rows (SM0..SM10) plus WS-RA, a findings catalogue (SMP-C1..C4, H1..H4, M/L), a §9 timeline table, and a §11 v1.0.0 acceptance checklist. Its §9 phase/version table also agrees with CLAUDE.md's table row-for-row apart from one version digit. What fails is the plan's job as a *status index*. Its header claim that "SM10 is the remaining phase" is not supported: five child plans — two of which name this very file as their "Parent overview" — appear nowhere in §5, §9 or Appendix A, and one of them (`SMP_FINE_LOCK_MIGRATION_PLAN.md`) carries two open items across 12 PRs including the deferred SM3.C.9 `withLockSet` migration that SM3's "CLOSED" row silently depends on. Separately, the overview scopes SM10 as "Documentation, tests, version closure" at ~500 LoC / 4-6 weeks while the phase plan shows SM10.1 additionally owes the bootable RPi5 image, the context-restore return-frame seam (`contextRestoreSeamLive := false` today) and cancellation error-frame staging — i.e. the entire basis of the v1.0.0 "bootable" headline. ### `docs/planning/SMP_PANIC_HANG_REMEDIATION_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: "**Status**: LANDED — the SM2.E remediation shipped the queued MCS-RW lock (`rust/sele4n-hal/src/queued_rw_lock.rs`) and closed both documented failure classes (see the SM2.E entry in `CHANGELOG.md`). The only remaining `panic!` sites in `queued_rw_lock.rs` are the deliberate panic-injection tests (guard-Drop release coverage), and no 'known flakiness under load' note survives in `rust/` or the p…
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

The plan's engineering *goal* — a panic-free, hang-free `queued_rw_lock.rs` — is genuinely achieved at HEAD, and I verified it by running the code: 26/26 module tests pass and 40 consecutive invocations of `queued_rw_lock::cross_thread_tests` were 40/40 green with zero hangs and zero panics. But the plan's *mechanism* claim is false. It says the remediation "shipped the queued MCS-RW lock" with a four-state `parked` machine, `WaiterSlot` chains, `signal_next_waiter` and `cascade_admit_readers`; that design deadlocked with the lock free at v0.32.147 and was deleted at v0.32.148 in favour of a ticket lock. Roughly two-thirds of this 1349-line plan (§1, §3.3, §5.1 Groups A1–A10, all of §6, §7.1.1–7.1.4, §9.1) describes machinery that no longer exists, including three named test pins that were removed with it. Separately, four named deliverables never landed and are registered nowhere: §6.7's reachable-state proof obligation (the queued variant has no Lean refinement bridge at all), §9.2's `Locks/Refinement.lean` methodology hub, §7.3's two `refinementMethodology*` anchors, and §9.4's GitBook chapter 16. Three of the six Definition-of-Done gates are literally unrunnable at HEAD (`cargo test --release` fails to compile on a deliberate `panic="abort"` guard; `ITER_OVERRIDE` is implemented nowhere; TSAN has no wiring). None of this blocks SM10: `queued_rw_lock` has no callers, no FFI exports and no Lean bindings, and the substantive structural defenses (build.rs scanner, FFI symmetry gate, 22-theorem count witness) are all current and green.

**Tree evidence checked:**

- rust/sele4n-hal/src/queued_rw_lock.rs (acquire_read) — the as-built ticket protocol: acquire_read takes a ticket, awaits its turn, fetch_adds the reader count and passes the turn on at entry; acquire_write awaits its turn then spins on compare_exchange(0, WRITER_BIT) at (scan_boot_s_for_per_cpu_data_setup) — writer admission is a CAS from exactly zero, never fetch_or, exactly as Group A9 required and as build.rs still forbids.
- Ran `cargo test -p sele4n-hal --lib queued_rw_lock`: `test result: ok. 26 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored`. Then ran 40 consecutive invocations of `queued_rw_lock::cross_thread_tests`: all 40 reported `12 passed; 0 failed`, zero timeouts, zero `panicked at`. The headline test `cross_thread_state_invariant_no_writer_with_readers` (rust/sele4n-hal/src/queued_rw_lock.rs (cross_thread_state_invariant_no_writer_with_readers)) passes every run, and its observer thread checks the mutex invariant…
- rust/sele4n-hal/src/queued_rw_lock.rs — `grep -n 'panic!'` returns exactly two hits, (cross_thread_panic_safety_writer_releases_on_unwind) and (cross_thread_panic_safety_reader_releases_on_unwind), both inside the deliberate panic-injection unwind tests. The header's panic-site claim is accurate. The four remaining hard failures are `assert!((core_id as usize) < MAX_WAITERS)` at (acquire_read), (release_read), (acquire_write), (release_write) — the §3.2 fail-closed class, each covered by a `should_panic` test at (out_of_range_core_id_acquire_read_panics).
- rust/sele4n-hal/build.rs (scan_queued_rw_lock_protocol_intact) `scan_queued_rw_lock_protocol_intact` is current with the tree, not stale: its docstring ( (scan_ffi_rs_exposes_timer_shadow_advance_export)) explicitly records that the MCS parked-constant and stale-self patterns were removed rather than weakened, and it now pins `self.now_serving.fetch_add(1` + `self.next_ticket.fetch_add(1` and forbids `self.state.fetch_or(WRITER_BIT`. Registered at rust/sele4n-hal/build.rs (main).
- `bash scripts/check_lock_ffi_symmetry.sh` runs green: "OK: both sides report SM2 theorem count = 22" and "SM2.D FFI surface is symmetric (16 symbols verified)". LOCK_THEOREM_COUNT = 22 at rust/sele4n-hal/src/lock_bridge.rs (LOCK_THEOREM_COUNT), matching SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/LockPrimitives.lean (lockPrimitives_count) `lockPrimitives_count : lockPrimitives.length = 22`. The §2 DoD bullet on the count witness holds.
- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/LockPrimitives.lean (lockPrimitives_identifiers_nodup) `lockPrimitives_identifiers_nodup` and (_rg_shim_cleanup) (descriptions Nodup) exist and are anchored in scripts/test_tier3_invariant_surface.sh (_rg_shim_cleanup) alongside `lockPrimitives_count` and the four partition counts. The §2 DoD bullet "NoDup-checked identifiers all resolve" is satisfied.
- §3.2's fail-closed inventory checks out exactly: rust/sele4n-hal/src/lock_bridge.rs has precisely 15 `panic!` sites, each with a "Panics if `handle` does not decode to a valid pool index" docstring line ( (switchToThreadHw_rejects_unencodable), (coreIdTargetMask), (sendRescheduleSgi), (coreIdTargetMask_bootCore), (dedupFold_mem), (dedupFold_nodup_cores), (shootdownAllAckedForRound)…); SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Runtime.lean (currentCoreId) is the `currentCoreId` totality-witness panic with the documented post-boot-invariant rationale at (currentCoreId).
- §7.6's documentation reinforcement is satisfied in substance even though the literal strings the plan specified are absent: rust/sele4n-hal/src/trap.rs (register_reschedule_sgi_handler) documents `handle_serror`'s `-> !` halt with the AK5-K/AK10 rationale, and rust/sele4n-hal/src/smp.rs (bring_up_secondaries_with_limit_inner), (MAX_CORE_COUNT), (MAX_CORE_COUNT) document the three `rust_secondary_main` halt loops at (rust_secondary_main), (rust_secondary_main) and (lean_secondary_kernel_main).

- The tests are actually gated on every push, not merely runnable by hand: scripts/test_rust.sh (run_cargo_step) runs `cargo test --all --features std`, and test_rust.sh is invoked from scripts/test_smoke.sh (Tier 2) and.github/workflows/lean_action_ci.yml. I also ran the exact Tier-1 lint gate `cargo clippy --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings` (scripts/test_rust.sh (run_cargo_step)) — clean.

- **Claimed**: "LANDED (v0.31.37) — per-core Vector state, SchedulerState, register banks, invariant migration, idle bootstrap (SM4.C.11 liveness-form migration is Scheduler-subsystem scope)"
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

SM4's core engineering claim — the path-a Vector replacement — is real and verifiable at HEAD. All seven `SchedulerState` fields are `Vector α numCores`, all seven accessors and seven setters exist, `default_state_perCoreInitialized` and `ext_perCore` (plan Theorems 3.6.1 / 3.3.1) are proven exactly as specified, the six `PerCoreVector` lemmas are in `Prelude.lean`, `bootFromPlatform_singleCore_witness` is genuinely deleted (surviving only in retirement notes), `bootFromPlatform_smp_witness` + `smpRetiredInventory` (8 entries, `_count`/`_covers_latent`/disposition partition) exist, the SM4.D six staged modules and SM4.C.29/.30 aggregates exist, and I confirmed live that the tier-2 trace is byte-identical and the SM4 modules build clean with no `sorry`/`axiom`. What is not finished is the integration edge. The three SM4.G items §3.7 explicitly deferred to SM5 are all still open at HEAD, SM5 has since closed without addressing any of them, and none is registered outside this already-LANDED plan — most consequentially, `bootFromPlatformChecked` (the production boot path) still builds plain `bootFromPlatform`, so no core gets an idle TCB, `enqueueIdleThreadOnCore` has no production caller, and SM5.J's `schedulerNoStall_smp` / `no_starvation_under_smp` rest on an `idleThreadEnqueuedOnCore` precondition the shipping boot path never establishes. That is a direct SM10 prerequisite. Separately, the SM4.C.11 registration is stale in both directions: most of the named Liveness predicates were in fact migrated by SM5.J, while the real residual — the bootCoreId-pinned `stepPrecondition`/`stepPost`/`ValidTrace` step relation — is not named anywhere, and the CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX entry claiming the trace model was "generalised to an arbitrary (c : CoreId) in production" overstates what landed. Two acceptance-gate boxes are genuinely unmet and five more are satisfied-but-unticked.

### `docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: No STATUS header. Header: "Audited cut: v0.31.2; Target releases: v0.98.0 → v1.0.0; Calendar estimate 4-6 weeks; 25-35 sub-tasks across ~10-15 PRs". §1 asserts "All substantive SMP work is complete; SM10 synchronizes documentation, completes the test suites, bumps the version, and records WS-SM closure." CLAUDE.md's phase table lists SM10 as PENDING.
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

SM10 has not started, so this is a readiness audit rather than a completeness audit. The good news dominates the prerequisite question: every obligation SM10 §2 names is real, durably registered in more than one place, and honestly scoped. WS-RA's staging half is genuinely landed (`blockedReturn_staged_in_waiter_frame`, SeLe4n/Kernel/Architecture/SyscallReturn.lean (blockedReturn_staged_in_waiter_frame)); the cancellation/timeout error-frame debt is registered with an owner in SYSCALL_RETURN_ABI_PLAN.md §9; the `lean_kernel_main` install-ordering obligation is cross-registered in the plan, `SeLe4n/Platform/FFI.lean (updateKernelState)` and `rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs`; the `lean_ready` gate makes the un-flipped seams structurally unreachable rather than hopefully dormant; version sync passes across 36 sites; and there is zero `sorry` and zero `axiom` in the comment-free code view. The problem is scope, not registration. §1's framing — "all substantive SMP work is complete", 4-6 weeks of documentation/test/version closure — is false against the tree: there is no bootable binary target anywhere in `rust/`, `lean_kernel_main` does not exist, `mark_lean_ready` has no production caller so no core ever enters the Lean runtime from hardware, and `contextRestoreSeamLive = false` keeps three scheduler dispatch paths provably inert. That seam's own docstring names three engineering prerequisites for the flip that appear nowhere in SM10's dependencies, sub-tasks or acceptance gate. Two claims in the §5 release-note template are false at HEAD (zero `native_decide`; per-object fine locks shipping), the sub-task tables are stale against a v0.31.2 baseline (5 of 6 named suites already exist), and the acceptance gate's "QEMU release-validation green" is vacuously satisfiable because all 14 QEMU sub-tests self-skip. SM10 can start, but not as written.

### `docs/planning/SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: Title marks the whole plan "post-v1.0.0"; §1 table (there) marks D-1/D-2/D-3/D-5/D-6 **LANDED** and D-4 **PARTIAL** ("the full trace-based theorem remains open"), with the header saying the work was "substantively delivered early in the pre-v0.31.10 SM2.C-defer cut" and only "D-4 partial and the D-1.9 full transition-edge theorem" remaining. §5.4 there separately claims D-4's "FULL main…
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

This plan is the post-v1.0.0 hardening path for the verified RwLock (D-1 temporal FIFO, D-2 writer wait bound, D-3 liveness, D-4 bisimulation, D-5 queued Rust impl, D-6 Tier-5 cross-language harness). Its Lean half is genuinely strong and better than the plan admits: `RwLockExecution`/`RwLockReachable`, `writerWaitDepth_bounded`, `writerWaitDepth_monotone_under_effective_release`, `rwLock_fifo_admission_temporal` (landed as the full D-1.9 theorem the §1 table still says "remains") and `rwLock_writer_liveness` with a `d × (maxDelay+1)` bound are all present, machine-proven, sorry/axiom-free, Tier-3 anchored and Tier-2 executed. The Rust/harness half is where the completeness claims do not survive contact with the tree: D-6's "Rust oracle" is a hand-written Rust transliteration of the Lean `applyOp` semantics rather than the real `rw_lock.rs`, i.e. exactly the STUB hazard the plan's own §5.6 says it closes; D-4's landed theorem chains a `ListBlockBisim` hypothesis whose `cons` premise is literally its own per-step conclusion, and the corollary ignores its `rustImplementsRwLock` argument; D-5's mandatory miri and loom acceptance gates do not exist anywhere in the repo, and the shipped `queued_rw_lock.rs` is a ticket lock that replaced the MCS design the plan still presents as adopted (§5.5) and whose failure modes §9 still lists as "eliminated by design". None of this blocks SM10: the RwLock's runtime surface (`SeLe4n.Kernel.Concurrency.LockBridge`) is a *staged* module (allowlist there), kernel entry is serialised by the global ticket lock in `rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs`, and the fine-lock model-level status is already registered in CLAUDE.md via SM3.C.9. The plan is therefore legitimately deferrable past v1.0.0 — but it is not "done", and its residue is not registered anywhere durable outside the plan file itself.

**Tree evidence checked:**

- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/RwLock.lean (rwLock_fifo_admission_temporal) — `rwLock_fifo_admission_temporal` exists as a real proven theorem (docstring: 'D-1.9 (FULL MAIN THEOREM)'), quantified over `RwLockExecution` with `h_initial_unheld`, the strict-transition `enqueueStep`/`admissionStep`, and an added `p₂ < a₂` precondition documented as an implement-the-improvement refinement.
- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/RwLock.lean (RwLockKernelStep)/2480/2533 — `RwLockKernelStep`, `RwLockReachable`, and the `RwLockExecution` structure with an `initial_reachable` field all landed exactly as §4.1 specifies; `RwLockReachable_implies_wf` at 2512 and `RwLockExecution.stateAt_reachable`/`stateAt_wf` at 2590+ are present.
- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/RwLock.lean (writerWaitDepth) `writerWaitDepth` + (writerWaitDepth_bounded) `writerWaitDepth_bounded` (tight `numCores - 1`) + (writerWaitDepth_monotone_under_effective_release) `writerWaitDepth_monotone_under_effective_release` + (RwLockState.isEffectiveRelease) `RwLockState.isEffectiveRelease` — D-2.1/.3/.4 are real, and the bound is writer-specific, not the old alias of `rwLock_bounded_wait_read`.
- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/RwLock.lean (rwLock_writer_liveness) — `rwLock_writer_liveness` is proven with the substantive `k_enq + d*(maxDelay+1)` bound under `FairTrace`, and the docstring honestly records the bound discrepancy from the plan's `d*maxDelay` rather than silently restating the plan.
- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/RwLock.lean (FairTrace) `FairTrace` (transition-edge form, both conjuncts, matching §4.5) + (FairTrace.decidable) `FairTrace.decidable` instance + (MAX_RELEASE_DELAY) `MAX_RELEASE_DELAY = 1024` — the D-3.1/.2/.7 gate items are all satisfied.
- SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/Locks/RwLock.lean (RwLockState.applyOp) — `RwLockState.applyOp` genuinely implements the §5.3 strict-FIFO structural fix (`else if s.writerHeld.isSome ∨ s.waiters ≠ []` for reads; `∨ s.readers ≠ []` added for writes), with the rationale in-code.
- Zero `sorry` and zero `axiom` across `SeLe4n/` and `tests/` (grep for `sorry` and `^axiom ` returns nothing) — the plan's §8 'axiom budget 0/0' holds at HEAD.
- scripts/test_tier3_invariant_surface.sh (_rg_shim_cleanup) — ~40 `#check` surface anchors for the D-1..D-4 deferred surface, including `rwLock_fifo_admission_temporal`, `FairTrace`, `opCorresponds`, `rustImplementsRwLock`, `concreteApplyOp_fetch_sub_no_underflow`, and the D-3.6 strict-FIFO foundations; Tier-3 anchor gate is real.
- tests/RwLockDeferredSuite.lean (457 lines, 27 `by decide` fixtures) is wired into Tier 2 at scripts/test_tier2_negative.sh (`lake exe rw_lock_deferred_suite`), so the deferred surface is exercised by `test_smoke.sh`, not only by nightly.
- scripts/test_tier5_cross_language.sh exists (133 lines), defaults to `TIER5_NUM_SEQUENCES=1000`, emits 10 structured edge cases plus deterministic pseudo-random sequences, and is invoked from scripts/test_nightly.sh under `NIGHTLY_ENABLE_EXPERIMENTAL=1` — the D-6 wiring and sequence-count gate are met.

### `docs/planning/SMP_VERIFIED_LOCK_PRIMITIVES_PLAN.md`

- **Claimed**: LANDED (v0.31.9) — memory model, TicketLock, RwLock, FFI bridge, refinement (SM2.C deferred items closed via SMP_RWLOCK_DEFERRED_COMPLETION_PLAN.md; SM2.E panic-hang remediation landed)
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

SM2 (verified lock primitives) is overwhelmingly real: all 22 named theorems in the plan's §10 catalogue exist as `theorem` declarations in code (not comments), all SM2.A/B/C definitions exist, the memory model / TicketLock / RwLock modules build clean at HEAD, the Rust `ticket_lock.rs` (962 LoC) and `rw_lock.rs` (1244 LoC) implementations with their RAII guards exist and carry dozens of cargo tests, the SM2.D.5 cross-language FFI symmetry gate runs green in Tier 0, the Tier-5 correspondence harness runs green (1000 sequences), and the repo-wide `sorry`/`axiom` count over the comment-free code view is zero. Three substantive gaps remain, all on the TicketLock/impl-wiring side. First, F-01 `rust_ticketLock_refines_lean` is not the operational simulation the plan promises: it relates only two `UInt64` counters, never mentions `applyOp`, never mentions `MemoryTrace`, and its fourth conjunct is a tautology — while its RwLock counterpart got a genuine trace-based bisimulation. Second, the Tier-5 differential harness covers RwLock only; there is no TicketLock oracle, so the primitive that serialises every kernel entry has neither a real refinement theorem nor a cross-language test. Third, `queued_rw_lock.rs` — the 1197-line FIFO-preserving lock built specifically to close the documented RwLock FIFO divergence — has zero exports and zero consumers, while the FFI pool Lean actually reaches still uses the non-FIFO CAS lock. Add to that three undelivered SM2.E documentation sub-tasks, a never-delivered "which kernel paths rely on FIFO" obligation that SM3 closed without, and an §8 acceptance gate whose eight checkboxes are all unticked with no aggregate closure CHANGELOG entry. None of this blocks SM10 from starting and nothing found is unsound, but the plan is not finished as its header claims.

### `improvement`

- **Claimed**: Cross-cutting sweep (no single plan header). The surfaces audited are claimed complete: SM5 per-core scheduler "Status: LANDED (v0.31.38 → v0.31.64)", WS-AN portfolio "COMPLETE (v0.30.11)" including AN9 hardware binding, AJ3 Platform &amp; Boot Pipeline "COMPLETE (v0.28.0)" in docs/CLAIM_EVIDENCE_INDEX.md, and README "Hardware binding | H3 COMPLETE". In-source docstrings on both the Lean and Rust…
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

The kernel surface is mostly honest and disciplined: zero sorry/axiom across the comment-free code view, all 67 Lean `@[extern]` symbols resolve to real Rust `#[no_mangle]` exports (empty diff both directions for the extern seam), the fail-closed shootdown halts are genuinely wired through `haltFailClosed` → `fatalHaltAll`, the cross-core SGI wrappers have real production consumers, and the hardware context-restore gap is honestly centralised behind one `contextRestoreSeamLive := false` flag that names its three prerequisites. The sweep nevertheless found a coherent cluster of implement-the-improvement violations concentrated in one place: the **Lean → HAL actuation layer**. `switchToThreadHw`, `perCoreCurrentThreadHw`, `idleWait`/`idleWaitBounded`, the whole MMIO/GIC/timer FFI group and `DeviceTree.fromDtbFull` are all defined, proven with structural-marker theorems, and documented as wired — yet every one has zero production consumers, so the verified decisions never reach hardware. Three of these sit directly on the SM10 bootability path (the scheduler's chosen thread never reaches the HAL's current-thread slot; idle cores never park; the `dtb_ptr` Rust already passes has no verified parser to reach), and none of the three is registered anywhere in `docs/planning/SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md`, whose only registered SM10.1 obligation is the `lean_kernel_main` install *ordering*. Separately, `SeLe4n/Platform/RPi5/MmioAdapter.lean` carries three deferred items that explicitly self-declare "no currently-active plan file tracks it", which the project's own debt-register rule forbids. SM10 can start, but it will discover these as unplanned work rather than as scheduled sub-tasks.

**Tree evidence checked:**

- Zero `sorry` and zero `axiom` across the comment-free overlay of SeLe4n/ + Main.lean (scripts/lean_code_view.py --overlay, then grep) — the hard project invariant holds.
- FFI extern direction is fully symmetric: 67 Lean `@[extern "..."]` symbols extracted from the overlay vs 72 Rust `#[no_mangle] pub extern "C" fn` exports; `comm -23` yields an empty set (no Lean extern lacks a Rust body).
- Reverse direction is accounted for: the 5 Rust exports without a Lean extern are `rust_boot_main`, `rust_secondary_main`, `handle_irq_per_core`, `handle_synchronous_exception`, `handle_serror` — all legitimately Rust-internal entry points, not orphans.
- 5 of the 6 real Lean `@[export]` symbols have matching Rust `extern "C"` declarations (`lean_syscall_dispatch_cross_core` rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (lean_syscall_dispatch_cross_core), `lean_per_core_timer_tick` rust/sele4n-hal/src/timer.rs (lean_per_core_timer_tick), `lean_secondary_kernel_main` rust/sele4n-hal/src/smp.rs (lean_secondary_kernel_main), `lean_per_core_reschedule` trap.rs, `suspend_thread_cross_core` rust/sele4n-hal/src/ffi.rs (cache_ic_maintenance)).
- The apparent 7th export `lean_endpoint_call_cross_core` is comment-only text; the overlay confirms it is not a live `@[export]`, matching the Staged.lean note that the 2-arg seam was deliberately removed in PR #820.
- Fail-closed halting is genuinely wired, not aspirational: `haltFailClosed` (SeLe4n/Kernel/SyscallDispatchEntry.lean (haltFailClosed)) calls `Concurrency.fatalHaltAll` and is invoked at both shootdown barriers — round-lock acquire exhaustion ( (acquireShootdownRoundLockServicingSelf)) and ack-wait timeout ( (completeShootdownRounds)).
- The TLB-shootdown ack timeout correctly does NOT release the round lock before halting (SeLe4n/Kernel/SyscallDispatchEntry.lean (completeShootdownRounds)), quarantining the subsystem rather than proceeding against a stale TLB — a real fail-closed design, not a documented intention.
- Cross-core SGI actuation is genuinely wired: `fireCrossCoreSgis` has 7 and `emitWakeSgi` 6 production consumers outside Runtime.lean/FFI.lean; `perCoreTimerTickEntry` really does fire them after its commit.
- `ffiSyscallReturnFrame` is live in `syscallDispatchCrossCoreEntry` (SeLe4n/Kernel/SyscallDispatchEntry.lean (syscallDispatchCrossCoreEntry)) for both the ok and error arms, and the Rust SVC path fail-closed-poisons a blocked caller's frame with `blocked_resume_sentinel_regs` (trap.rs) exactly as CLAUDE.md describes.
- `contextRestoreSeamLive := false` (SeLe4n/Kernel/Concurrency/ContextRestoreSeam.lean (contextRestoreSeamLive)) is a single importless source of truth read by all three live guards, with its three blocking prerequisites named explicitly — honest registered debt, not a false claim.

### `security`

- **Claimed**: Cross-cutting security posture is implicitly claimed complete: WS-SM SM8 (per-core non-interference) and SM9 (declassification completion) are both marked CLOSED/LANDED in CLAUDE.md, WS-RA is COMPLETE, zero sorry/axiom is asserted as a hard invariant, and SM10 (the remaining phase before v1.0.0) is scoped in SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md as "Documentation, Tests, Version Closure" only — i.e. no sec…
- **Verified**: **incomplete**

The sweep found the proof surface, capability gating, ABI design and Rust unsafe discipline to be genuinely strong: zero sorry/axiom, a total syscall-to-right mapping, a proven "no dispatch without a rights check" theorem, the retired bit-63 protocol truly deleted, fail-closed userspace decoding, and 112 unsafe sites essentially all carrying soundness arguments. However, one critical defect undermines the entire information-flow story: the live labeling-context reference ships `testLabelingContext`, which labels every thread, object, endpoint and service with id != 0 as `publicLabel`, and no production labeling context exists anywhere in the tree. `bootAndInitialiseFromPlatform`'s context parameter defaults to `none`, its only callers are tests, and the `isInsecureDefaultContext` fail-closed guard is defeated by that policy because it probes only ids 0/1/42 and short-circuits on id 0 being kernelTrusted. Since `canFlow` is reflexive, every flow between user-visible entities is permitted, making SM8/SM9's verified non-interference and declassification enforcement vacuous in the shipped configuration — while a docstring asserts the boot wrapper installs a production policy on hardware. Separately, VM faults are unhandled: the data/instruction-abort arms set x0 and return without advancing ELR or delivering a fault, producing an infinite fault loop, registered only as a parenthetical source comment. Finally, the flagship capability-enforcement theorem covers only the legacy `syscallEntry`, not the live `syscallEntryChecked` path the hardware actually invokes. SM10 as currently scoped absorbs none of these.

**Tree evidence checked:**

- Zero sorry/axiom confirmed across SeLe4n/, tests/ and Main.lean by direct grep - the hard project invariant holds.
- `syscallRequiredRight` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (syscallRequiredRight)) is a total function over SyscallId with no catch-all arm, so a new syscall cannot be added without declaring its required right; `syscallRequiredRight_total` pins this.
- `dispatchSyscall_requires_right` (SeLe4n/Kernel/API.lean (dispatchSyscall_requires_right)) genuinely proves that a successful dispatch implies the caller held a capability with the required right, threading through `syscallInvoke_requires_right`.
- The rights-check-skipping `syscallInvokeResolved` is reachable only when `syscallChecksTargetFirst` is true, which `syscallChecksTargetFirst_iff` proves is exactly {auditRead, auditDrain}; both arms re-check `cap.hasRight gate.requiredRight` internally, pinned by `dispatchWithCapChecked_audit_insufficient_right_denied`.
- The retired bit-63 ABI is genuinely dead: `encodeOk`/`encodeError` are absent from the comment-free code view, and the 0x7FFFFFFFFFFFFFFF mask survives only inside the negative theorem `bit63Encoding_not_injective_on_badges` (SeLe4n/Kernel/Architecture/SyscallReturn.lean (bit63Encoding_not_injective_on_badges)). Its retention is sound - it is a witness preserving why the protocol was retired, not live code.
- The v2 error encoding is collision-free by construction: `error_frame_regs` (rust/sele4n-hal/src/svc_dispatch.rs (error_frame_regs)) uses the offset label `(disc+1)<<9` so discriminant 0 (InvalidCapability) cannot alias the success label 0, and `blocked_resume_sentinel_regs` uses the max in-field label so a premature resume decodes as UnknownKernelError rather than success.
- Userspace `decode_response` (rust/sele4n-abi/src/decode.rs) is fail-closed: over-wide x1 is rejected as InvalidMessageInfo rather than truncated, and unrecognized labels collapse to UnknownKernelError (still an Err).
- 112 unsafe sites across rust/; only 8 lack a nearby safety note and all 8 are either inside #[cfg(test)] functions or carry an equivalent soundness argument in adjacent prose (e.g. rust/sele4n-hal/src/gic.rs (register_sgi_handler)'s boot-time-only contract on SGI_HANDLERS).
- ARM64 decode is bounds-safe: `decodeSyscallArgs` calls `validateRegBound` on capPtrReg, msgInfoReg, syscallNumReg and every message register before indexing.
- The userspace-supplied `ipcBufferAddr` is deliberately ignored (`_ipcBufferAddr`) in `syscallDispatchFromAbi`; the IPC buffer is taken from the TCB, matching seL4 and avoiding an unvalidated user pointer.

## 10. Recommended sequence before SM10 opens

> **This sequence is now planned in full.** Every item below, plus the
> security findings of §4 and the medium sweep of §6, is decomposed into
> 155 PR-sized sub-tasks across nine phases in
> [`SMP_RELEASE_READINESS_PLAN.md`](SMP_RELEASE_READINESS_PLAN.md) (WS-RR).
> The §7 low-severity table is not enumerated in that plan; RR0.11 triages it
> by remedy, sending prose fixes to SM10.2's documentation sweep and anything
> needing code or a proof back into RR7 as a numbered row.


Ordered so each step unblocks the next. Steps 1–3 close the blockers;
steps 4–5 close the fail-open latents that become live exactly when the
boot seam flips.

1. **Register the orphaned workstream.** Add
 [`IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md`](../dev_history/planning/IPC_INVARIANT_DETHREADING_PLAN.md)
 to `docs/REGISTERED_DEBT.md` — the project's declared single
 canonical source for workstream status — with its real state
 (D0/D2/D2′/D3/D4/D5/D7 closed; D1, D6, D8 open), and to
 `SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` §2 Dependencies. Alternatively close
 D1/D6/D8. Either way, WS-SM must not close over it silently.
2. **Re-scope SM10.** Rewrite `SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` §1 and its
 estimate against §2.2 of this document; promote the boot seam to a
 first-class SM10.1 sub-task (aarch64 image target + Lean cross-compile
 + bare-metal runtime hosting + `@[export] lean_kernel_main` + the
 install-ordering resolution `rust/sele4n-hal/src/kernel_entry.rs` already demands);
 add the missing SM9 term and archive entry per §2.3.
3. **Fix the two live-path proof gaps** — cross-core donation's missing
 CBS replenish-queue migration, and the absent `ipcInvariantFull`
 preservation on the live `.send` arm. Both are implement-the-improvement
 cases whose groundwork is already staged.
4. **Close the fail-open latents before the boot path goes live** — the
 unhandled VM-fault loop, the all-public default labeling context, and
 the two kernel seams that skip the `lean_ready` gate. None is
 exploitable today because nothing boots; each becomes reachable the
 moment SM10.1 succeeds, which is the wrong time to discover them.
5. **Hand the acceptance-gate execution to SM10, where the image exists.**
 This step cannot run before SM10 opens: tier 4 under
 `SELE4N_REQUIRE_GATES=1` needs the bootable image that SM10.1.1 produces,
 so scheduling it inside a sequence that completes *before* SM10 would be
 circular. WS-RR's obligation is to leave the gate honest and the
 dependency visible; SM10.1 runs it immediately after D1 and before release
 acceptance. Until it runs, `SMP_RELEASE_CLOSURE_PLAN.md` §2's "acceptance
 gates for SM0..SM9 green" is unmet, not merely unverified — and that is a
 statement SM10 must satisfy, not one WS-RR can close.

The **§7** table is triaged by RR0.11 before anyone works it: most of its rows
are documentation drift and become SM10.2's work-list, but the table is not
uniformly doc-sync — rows classed `improvement`, `debt`, `gates` and `bootpath`
need code, proofs or wiring, and finding 8 is a `soundness` item that merely
carries low severity. Low means the consequence is small, not that the remedy
is a sentence, so those rows return to RR7 as numbered work or become
registered deferrals with owners.
The §6 mediums are **not** SM10.2's: WS-RR phase RR7 owns them and must close
or explicitly defer each one, so routing them into a documentation sweep would
let substantive remediations lapse while RR7 still read as complete. The §8
table records what was checked and cleared, so a later reader does not re-open
settled ground without new evidence.
